### Algebraic Expansion of Binomials

This section covers the expansion of binomial products, including the difference of two squares and the squaring of binomials.

---

#### a) Difference of Two Squares
**Problem:** $(g + 11)(g - 11)$
**Solution:**
$$(g + 11)(g - 11) = g^2 + 11g - 11g - 121$$
$$= g^2 - 121$$

#### b) Binomial Expansion
**Problem:** $(4b - 2)(2b - 4)$
**Solution:**
$$(4b - 2)(2b - 4) = 8b^2 - 16b - 4b + 8$$
$$= 8b^2 - 20b + 8$$

#### c) Binomial Expansion
**Problem:** $(4b - 3)(2b - 1)$
**Solution:**
$$(4b - 3)(2b - 1) = 8b^2 - 4b - 6b + 3$$
$$= 8b^2 - 10b + 3$$

#### d) Binomial Expansion
**Problem:** $(6x - 4)(3x + 6)$
**Solution:**
$$(6x - 4)(3x + 6) = 18x^2 + 36x - 12x - 24$$
$$= 18x^2 + 24x - 24$$

#### e) Binomial Expansion
**Problem:** $(3w - 2)(2w + 7)$
**Solution:**
$$(3w - 2)(2w + 7) = 6w^2 + 21w - 4w - 14$$
$$= 6w^2 + 17w - 14$$

#### f) Squaring a Binomial
**Problem:** $(2t - 3)^2$
**Solution:**
$$(2t - 3)^2 = (2t - 3)(2t - 3)$$
$$= 4t^2 - 6t - 6t + 9$$
$$= 4t^2 - 12t + 9$$

#### g) Squaring a Binomial
**Problem:** $(5p - 8)^2$
**Solution:**
$$(5p - 8)^2 = (5p - 8)(5p - 8)$$
$$= 25p^2 - 40p - 40p + 64$$
$$= 25p^2 - 80p + 64$$

#### h) Squaring a Binomial
**Problem:** $(4y + 5)^2$
**Solution:**
$$(4y + 5)^2 = (4y + 5)(4y + 5)$$
$$= 16y^2 + 20y + 20y + 25$$
$$= 16y^2 + 40y + 25$$

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Multiplication of Polynomials

This section covers the expansion of algebraic expressions involving the multiplication of monomials, binomials, and trinomials.

---

#### Worked Examples

**i) $(2y^6 + 3y^5)(-5y - 12)$**
$$(2y^6 + 3y^5)(-5y - 12) = -10y^7 - 24y^6 - 15y^6 - 36y^5$$
$$= -10y^7 - 39y^6 - 36y^5$$

**j) $9(8y^2 - 2y + 3)$**
$$9(8y^2 - 2y + 3) = 72y^2 - 18y + 27$$

**k) $(-2y^2 - 4y + 11)(5y - 12)$**
$$(-2y^2 - 4y + 11)(5y - 12) = -10y^3 - 20y^2 + 55y + 24y^2 + 48y - 132$$
$$= -10y^3 + 4y^2 + 103y - 132$$

**l) $(7y^2 - 6y - 8)(-2y + 2)$**
$$(7y^2 - 6y - 8)(-2y + 2) = -14y^3 + 12y^2 + 16y + 14y^2 - 12y - 16$$
$$= -14y^3 + 26y^2 + 4y - 16$$

**m) $(10y + 3)(-2y^2 - 11y + 2)$**
$$(10y + 3)(-2y^2 - 11y + 2) = -20y^3 - 110y^2 + 20y - 6y^2 - 33y + 6$$
$$= -20y^3 - 116y^2 - 13y + 6$$

**n) $(-12y - 3)(2y^2 - 11y + 3)$**
$$(-12y - 3)(2y^2 - 11y + 3) = -24y^3 + 132y^2 - 36y - 6y^2 + 33y - 9$$
$$= -24y^3 + 126y^2 - 3y - 9$$

**o) $(-10)(2y^2 + 8y + 3)$**
$$(-10)(2y^2 + 8y + 3) = -20y^2 - 80y - 30$$

**p) $(7y + 3)(7y^2 + 3y + 10)$**
$$(7y + 3)(7y^2 + 3y + 10) = 49y^3 + 21y^2 + 70y + 21y^2 + 9y + 30$$
$$= 49y^3 + 42y^2 + 79y + 30$$

**q) $(a + 2b)(a^2 + b^2 + 2ab)$**
$$(a + 2b)(a^2 + b^2 + 2ab) = a^3 + ab^2 + 2a^2b + 2a^2b + 2b^3 + 4ab^2$$
$$= a^3 + 4a^2b + 5ab^2 + 2b^3$$

---

### Algebraic Expansion and Simplification

This section covers the expansion of algebraic expressions involving binomials, trinomials, and powers.

---

#### r) Expansion of $(x + y)(x^2 - xy + y^2)$
**Solution:**
$$(x + y)(x^2 - xy + y^2) = x^3 - x^2y + xy^2 + x^2y - xy^2 + y^3$$
$$= x^3 + y^3$$

---

#### s) Expansion of $3m(9m^2 + 2) + 5m^2(5m + 6)$
**Solution:**
$$3m(9m^2 + 2) + 5m^2(5m + 6) = 27m^3 + 6m + 25m^3 + 30m^2$$
$$= 52m^3 + 30m^2 + 6m$$

---

#### t) Expansion of $4x^2(10x^3 + 4) + 4x^3(2x^2 + 6)$
**Solution:**
$$4x^2(10x^3 + 4) + 4x^3(2x^2 + 6) = 40x^5 + 16x^2 + 8x^5 + 24x^3$$
$$= 48x^5 + 24x^3 + 16x^2$$

---

#### u) Expansion of $3k^3(k^2 + 3) + 2k^2(6k^3 + 7)$
**Solution:**
$$3k^3(k^2 + 3) + 2k^2(6k^3 + 7) = 3k^5 + 9k^3 + 12k^5 + 14k^2$$
$$= 15k^5 + 9k^3 + 14k^2$$

---

#### v) Expansion of $(3x + 2)(3x - 2)(9x^2 - 4)$
**Solution:**
$$(3x + 2)(3x - 2)(9x^2 - 4) = (9x^2 - 4)(9x^2 - 4)$$
$$= 81x^4 - 36x^2 - 36x^2 + 16$$
$$= 81x^4 - 72x^2 + 16$$

---

#### w) Expansion of $(-6y^4 + 11y^2 + 3y)(y + 4)(y - 4)$
**Solution:**
$$(-6y^4 + 11y^2 + 3y)(y + 4)(y - 4) = (-6y^4 + 11y^2 + 3y)(y^2 - 16)$$
$$= -6y^6 + 96y^4 + 11y^4 - 176y^2 + 3y^3 - 48y$$
$$= -6y^6 + 107y^4 + 3y^3 - 176y^2 - 48y$$

---

#### x) Expansion of $(x + 2)(x - 3)(x^2 + 2x - 3)$
**Solution:**
$$(x + 2)(x - 3)(x^2 + 2x - 3) = (x^2 - x - 6)(x^2 + 2x - 3)$$
$$= x^4 + 2x^3 - 3x^2 - x^3 - 2x^2 + 3x - 6x^2 - 12x + 18$$
$$= x^4 + x^3 - 11x^2 - 9x + 18$$

---

#### y) Expansion of $(a + 2)^2 - (2a - 4)^2$
**Solution:**
$$(a + 2)^2 - (2a - 4)^2 = (a^2 + 4a + 4) - (4a^2 - 16a + 16)$$
$$= a^2 + 4a + 4 - 4a^2 + 16a - 16$$
$$= -3a^2 + 20a - 12$$

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Expansion of Products

This section focuses on the expansion of algebraic expressions, involving the distribution of terms, squaring binomials, and simplifying polynomials.

---

#### Worked Examples

**a) Expand $(2x + 3)^2 - (x - 2)^2$**
$$(2x + 3)^2 - (x - 2)^2 = (4x^2 + 12x + 9) - (x^2 - 4x + 4)$$
$$= 4x^2 + 12x + 9 - x^2 + 4x - 4$$
$$= 3x^2 + 16x + 5$$

**b) Expand $(2a^2 - a - 1)(a^2 + 3a + 2)$**
$$(2a^2 - a - 1)(a^2 + 3a + 2) = 2a^4 + 6a^3 + 4a^2 - a^3 - 3a^2 - 2a - a^2 - 3a - 2$$
$$= 2a^4 + 5a^3 - 5a - 2$$

**c) Expand $(y^2 + 4y - 1)(1 - 4y - y^2)$**
$$(y^2 + 4y - 1)(1 - 4y - y^2) = y^2 - 4y^3 - y^4 + 4y - 16y^2 - 4y^3 - 1 + 4y + y^2$$
$$= -y^4 - 8y^3 - 14y^2 + 8y - 1$$

**d) Expand $2(x - 2y)(x^2 + xy + y^2)$**
$$2(x - 2y)(x^2 + xy + y^2) = 2(x^3 + x^2y + xy^2 - 2x^2y - 2xy^2 - y^3)$$
$$= 2(x^3 - x^2y - xy^2 - y^3)$$
$$= 2x^3 - 2x^2y - 2xy^2 - 2y^3$$

**e) Expand $3(a - 3b)(a^2 + 3ab - b^2)$**
$$3(a - 3b)(a^2 + 3ab - b^2) = 3(a^3 + 3a^2b - ab^2 - 3a^2b - 9ab^2 + 3b^3)$$
$$= 3(a^3 - 10ab^2 + 3b^3)$$
$$= 3a^3 - 30ab^2 + 9b^3$$

**f) Expand $(2a - b)(2a + b)(2a^2 - 3ab + b^2)$**
$$(2a - b)(2a + b)(2a^2 - 3ab + b^2) = (4a^2 - b^2)(2a^2 - 3ab + b^2)$$
$$= 8a^4 - 12a^3b + 4a^2b^2 - 2a^2b^2 + 3ab^3 - b^4$$
$$= 8a^4 - 12a^3b + 2a^2b^2 + 3ab^3 - b^4$$

**g) Expand $2(3x + y)(3x - y) - (3x - y)^2$**
$$2(3x + y)(3x - y) - (3x - y)^2 = 2(9x^2 - y^2) - (9x^2 - 6xy + y^2)$$
$$= 18x^2 - 2y^2 - 9x^2 + 6xy - y^2$$
$$= 9x^2 + 6xy - 3y^2$$

**h) Expand $(x + y)(x - 3y) + (2x - y)^2$**
$$(x + y)(x - 3y) + (2x - y)^2 = (x^2 - 3xy + xy - 3y^2) + (4x^2 - 4xy + y^2)$$
$$= x^2 - 2xy - 3y^2 + 4x^2 - 4xy + y^2$$
$$= 5x^2 - 6xy - 2y^2$$

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Expansion and Solving for Unknowns

#### 1. Expanding Binomials with Fractions
To expand expressions involving fractions, apply the distributive property (FOIL method) and find common denominators where necessary.

**Example i)**
$$\left(\frac{x}{3} - \frac{3}{x}\right)\left(\frac{x}{4} + \frac{4}{x}\right)$$
**Solution:**
$$\left(\frac{x}{3} - \frac{3}{x}\right)\left(\frac{x}{4} + \frac{4}{x}\right) = \frac{x^2}{12} + \frac{4}{3} - \frac{3}{4} - \frac{12}{x^2}$$
$$= \frac{x^2}{12} + \frac{16}{12} - \frac{9}{12} - \frac{12}{x^2}$$
$$= \frac{x^2}{12} + \frac{7}{12} - \frac{12}{x^2}$$

**Example j)**
$$\left(x - \frac{2}{x}\right)\left(\frac{x}{3} + \frac{4}{x}\right)$$
**Solution:**
$$\left(x - \frac{2}{x}\right)\left(\frac{x}{3} + \frac{4}{x}\right) = \frac{x^2}{3} + 4 - \frac{2}{3} - \frac{8}{x^2}$$
$$= \frac{x^2}{3} + \frac{12}{3} - \frac{2}{3} - \frac{8}{x^2}$$
$$= \frac{x^2}{3} + \frac{10}{3} - \frac{8}{x^2}$$

---

#### 2. Simplifying Algebraic Expressions
Distribute the scalar values into the brackets and combine like terms.

**Example k)**
$$\frac{1}{2}(10x - 12y) + \frac{1}{3}(15x - 18y)$$
**Solution:**
$$\frac{1}{2}(10x - 12y) + \frac{1}{3}(15x - 18y) = 5x - 6y + 5x - 6y$$
$$= 10x - 12y$$

**Example l)**
$$\frac{1}{2}a(4a + 6b) + \frac{1}{4}(8a + 12b)$$
**Solution:**
$$\frac{1}{2}a(4a + 6b) + \frac{1}{4}(8a + 12b) = 2a^2 + 3ab + 2a + 3b$$

---

#### 3. Solving for Unknown Constants
By expanding the given equation and comparing coefficients, we can determine the value of unknown variables.

**Question 4: Find $b$ in $(x + b)(x - 1) = x^2 + 3x - 4$**
**Solution:**
$$(x + b)(x - 1) = x^2 - x + bx - b$$
Comparing the constant term: $-b = -4$, therefore $b = 4$.
Verification: $x$ term is $-x + 4x = 3x$. This matches the original equation.

**Question 5: Find $g$ in $(x - 2)(x + g) = x^2 - 6x + 8$**
**Solution:**
$$(x - 2)(x + g) = x^2 + gx - 2x - 2g$$
Comparing the constant term: $-2g = 8$, therefore $g = -4$.
Verification: $x$ term is $-4x - 2x = -6x$. This matches the original equation.

---

#### 4. Analyzing Conditions for Variables
**Question 6: In $(x - 4)(x + k) = x^2 + bx + c$**
Expand the expression: $(x - 4)(x + k) = x^2 + kx - 4x - 4k = x^2 + (k - 4)x - 4k$.

**a) For which of these values of $k$ will $b$ be positive? (Values: $-3; -1; 0; 3; 5$)**
**Solution:**
The $x$ term coefficient is $b = k - 4$.
For $b > 0$, then $k - 4 > 0$, so $k > 4$.
From the given list, **$k = 5$** makes $b$ positive.

**b) For which of these values of $k$ will $c$ be positive? (Values: $-3; -1; 0; 3; 5$)**
**Solution:**
The constant term is $c = -4k$.
For $c > 0$, then $-4k > 0$, so $k < 0$.
From the given list, **$k = -3$** and **$k = -1$** make $c$ positive.

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Expansion and Substitution

#### Analysis of Quadratic Forms
When expanding expressions of the form $(x + k)$, we use the distributive property:
$$(x - 4)(x + k) = x^2 + kx - 4x - 4k$$

*   **Constant term:** The constant term is $-4k$.
*   **Condition for positive constant:** For the constant term to be positive, $k$ must be negative ($k < 0$).
*   **Condition for positive $b$ (coefficient of $x$):** Given the expression $x^2 + (k-4)x - 4k$, the coefficient of $x$ is $(k-4)$. For this to be positive, $k > 4$.

---

#### Worked Example 7: Expanding and Evaluating Expressions
**a) Expand $\left(x + \frac{4}{x}\right)^2$**

**Solution:**
$$\left(x + \frac{4}{x}\right)^2 = \left(x + \frac{4}{x}\right)\left(x + \frac{4}{x}\right)$$
$$= x^2 + 4 + 4 + \frac{16}{x^2}$$
$$= x^2 + 8 + \frac{16}{x^2}$$

**b) Given $\left(x + \frac{4}{x}\right)^2 = 14$, determine the value of $x^2 + \frac{16}{x^2}$ without solving for $x$.**

**Solution:**
From (a), we know:
$$\left(x + \frac{4}{x}\right)^2 = x^2 + 8 + \frac{16}{x^2}$$
Substitute the given value $14$:
$$14 = x^2 + 8 + \frac{16}{x^2}$$
Subtract $8$ from both sides:
$$14 - 8 = x^2 + \frac{16}{x^2}$$
$$6 = x^2 + \frac{16}{x^2}$$

---

#### Worked Example 8: Algebraic Manipulation
**a) Expand $\left(a + \frac{1}{a}\right)^2$**

**Solution:**
$$\left(a + \frac{1}{a}\right)^2 = a^2 + 2(a)\left(\frac{1}{a}\right) + \frac{1}{a^2}$$
$$= a^2 + 2 + \frac{1}{a^2}$$

**b) Given $\left(a + \frac{1}{a}\right) = 3$, determine the value of $\left(a + \frac{1}{a}\right)^2$ without solving for $a$.**

**Solution:**
$$\left(a + \frac{1}{a}\right)^2 = 3^2$$
$$= 9$$

**c) Given $\left(a - \frac{1}{a}\right) = 3$, determine the value of $\left(a + \frac{1}{a}\right)^2$ without solving for $a$.**

**Solution:**
*Note: This requires relating the square of the difference to the square of the sum.*

---

### Algebraic Identities and Expansion

#### Key Concepts
The expansion of binomials involving reciprocals follows the standard algebraic identities:
1. $(a + b)^2 = a^2 + 2ab + b^2$
2. $(a - b)^2 = a^2 - 2ab + b^2$

When dealing with reciprocals, the middle term $2ab$ simplifies because the variables cancel out. For example:
$$\left(a + \frac{1}{a}\right)^2 = a^2 + 2(a)\left(\frac{1}{a}\right) + \frac{1}{a^2} = a^2 + 2 + \frac{1}{a^2}$$
$$\left(a - \frac{1}{a}\right)^2 = a^2 - 2(a)\left(\frac{1}{a}\right) + \frac{1}{a^2} = a^2 - 2 + \frac{1}{a^2}$$

**Relationship Note:**
If we add $4$ to $\left(a - \frac{1}{a}\right)^2$, we obtain $\left(a + \frac{1}{a}\right)^2$:
$$\left(a - \frac{1}{a}\right)^2 + 4 = \left(a^2 - 2 + \frac{1}{a^2}\right) + 4 = a^2 + 2 + \frac{1}{a^2} = \left(a + \frac{1}{a}\right)^2$$

---

#### Exercises and Worked Examples

**9. Expansion and Evaluation**

**a) Expand:** $\left(3y + \frac{1}{2y}\right)^2$
**Solution:**
$$\left(3y + \frac{1}{2y}\right)^2 = (3y)^2 + 2(3y)\left(\frac{1}{2y}\right) + \left(\frac{1}{2y}\right)^2 = 9y^2 + 3 + \frac{1}{4y^2}$$

**b) Given that $3y + \frac{1}{2y} = 4$, determine the value of $\left(3y + \frac{1}{2y}\right)^2$ without solving for $y$.**
**Solution:**
$$\left(3y + \frac{1}{2y}\right)^2 = (4)^2 = 16$$

---

**10. Expansion and Evaluation**

**a) Expand:** $\left(a + \frac{1}{3a}\right)^2$
**Solution:**
$$\left(a + \frac{1}{3a}\right)^2 = a^2 + 2(a)\left(\frac{1}{3a}\right) + \left(\frac{1}{3a}\right)^2 = a^2 + \frac{2}{3} + \frac{1}{9a^2}$$

**b) Expand:** $\left(a + \frac{1}{3a}\right)\left(a^2 - \frac{1}{3} + \frac{1}{9a^2}\right)$
**Solution:**
$$\left(a + \frac{1}{3a}\right)\left(a^2 - \frac{1}{3} + \frac{1}{9a^2}\right) = a^3 - \frac{1}{3}a + \frac{1}{9a} + \frac{1}{3}a - \frac{1}{9a} + \frac{1}{27a^3} = a^3 + \frac{1}{27a^3}$$

**c) Given that $a + \frac{1}{3a} = 2$, determine the value of $a^3 + \frac{1}{27a^3}$ without solving for $a$.**
**Solution:**
Using the result from (b):
$$\left(a + \frac{1}{3a}\right)\left(a^2 - \frac{1}{3} + \frac{1}{9a^2}\right) = a^3 + \frac{1}{27a^3}$$
Since $a + \frac{1}{3a} = 2$, we substitute:
$$2 \left(a^2 - \frac{1}{3} + \frac{1}{9a^2}\right) = a^3 + \frac{1}{27a^3}$$
*(Note: Further evaluation requires the value of $a^2 + \frac{1}{9a^2}$, which can be derived from squaring the given expression).*

---

### 1.7 Factorisation: Common Factors

Factorisation is the process of writing an algebraic expression as a product of its factors. The simplest method of factorisation is identifying and taking out the **highest common factor (HCF)** from each term in the expression.

---

#### Worked Examples: Factorising by taking out a common factor

**Example 1**
Factorise: $12x + 32y$
*   **Step 1:** Identify the HCF of $12$ and $32$, which is $4$.
*   **Step 2:** Divide each term by $4$ and place the $4$ outside the brackets.
*   **Solution:** 
$$12x + 32y = 4(3x + 8y)$$

**Example 2**
Factorise: $-2ab^2 - 4a^2b$
*   **Step 1:** Identify the HCF of the coefficients ($-2$ and $-4$ is $-2$) and the variables ($a$ and $b$). The HCF is $-2ab$.
*   **Step 2:** Divide each term by $-2ab$.
*   **Solution:** 
$$-2ab^2 - 4a^2b = -2ab(b + 2a)$$

**Example 3**
Factorise: $18ab - 3bc$
*   **Step 1:** Identify the HCF of $18$ and $3$ ($3$) and the common variable ($b$). The HCF is $3b$.
*   **Step 2:** Divide each term by $3b$.
*   **Solution:** 
$$18ab - 3bc = 3b(6a - c)$$

**Example 4**
Factorise: $12kj + 18kq$
*   **Step 1:** Identify the HCF of $12$ and $18$ ($6$) and the common variable ($k$). The HCF is $6k$.
*   **Step 2:** Divide each term by $6k$.
*   **Solution:** 
$$12kj + 18kq = 6k(2j + 3q)$$

---

#### Advanced Algebraic Identity Example
The following example demonstrates the application of algebraic identities to evaluate expressions:

Given: $a^3 + \frac{1}{27a^3}$
Using the sum of cubes identity:
$$a^3 + \frac{1}{27a^3} = \left(a + \frac{1}{3a}\right) \left(a^2 - \frac{1}{3} + \frac{1}{9a^2}\right)$$
$$= 2 \left(a^2 - \frac{1}{3} + \frac{1}{9a^2}\right)$$

Where:
$$a^2 - \frac{1}{3} + \frac{1}{9a^2} = \left(a + \frac{1}{3a}\right)^2 - 1$$
$$= 4 - 1 = 3$$

Therefore:
$$a^3 + \frac{1}{27a^3} = 2(3) = 6$$

<!-- DIAGRAM_PLACEHOLDER: diagram -->

---

### Factorisation Exercises

This section focuses on the algebraic technique of factorisation by taking out the highest common factor (HCF) and applying difference of squares.

---

#### Worked Examples

**5. Factorise: $-12a + 24a^3$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$-12a + 24a^3 = 12a(-1 + 2a^2)$$

**6. Factorise: $-2ab - 8a$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$-2ab - 8a = -2a(b + 4)$$

**7. Factorise: $24kj - 16k^2j$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$24kj - 16k^2j = 8kj(3 - 2k)$$

**8. Factorise: $-a^2b - b^2a$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$-a^2b - b^2a = -ab(a + b)$$

**9. Factorise: $72b^2q - 18b^3q^2$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$72b^2q - 18b^3q^2 = 18b^2q(4 - bq)$$

**10. Factorise: $125x^6 - 5y^2$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$125x^6 - 5y^2 = 5(25x^6 - y^2)$$
    $$= 5(5x^3 - y)(5x^3 + y)$$

**11. Factorise: $6x^2 + 2x + 10x^3$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$6x^2 + 2x + 10x^3 = 2x(3x + 1 + 5x^2)$$

**12. Factorise: $2xy^2 + xy^2z + 3xy$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$2xy^2 + xy^2z + 3xy = xy(2y + yz + 3)$$

**13. Factorise: $12k^2j + 24k^2j^2$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$12k^2j + 24k^2j^2 = 12k^2j(1 + 2j)$$

**14. Factorise: $3a^2 + 6a - 18$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$3a^2 + 6a - 18 = 3(a^2 + 2a - 6)$$

**15. Factorise: $7a + 4$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$7a + 4$$
    *(Note: This expression cannot be factorised further as there are no common factors other than 1.)*

---

# Algebraic Expressions: Factorisation by Grouping

## Concept Overview
Factorisation by grouping involves identifying a common binomial factor within an expression. Sometimes, you may need to manipulate the signs within a bracket (e.g., changing $(3-y)$ to $-(y-3)$) to reveal a common factor that can be extracted.

---

## Worked Examples

### Exercise 1: Factorising with sign manipulation
**Problem:** $4(y - 3) + k(3 - y)$

**Solution:**
1. Change $(3 - y)$ to $-(y - 3)$ to create a common factor:
   $$4(y - 3) - k(y - 3)$$
2. Factor out the common binomial $(y - 3)$:
   $$(y - 3)(4 - k)$$

### Exercise 2: Difference of two squares
**Problem:** $a^2(a - 1) - 25(a - 1)$

**Solution:**
1. Factor out the common binomial $(a - 1)$:
   $$(a - 1)(a^2 - 25)$$
2. Factor the difference of two squares $(a^2 - 25)$:
   $$(a - 1)(a - 5)(a + 5)$$

### Exercise 3: Common factor extraction
**Problem:** $bm(b + 4) - 6m(b + 4)$

**Solution:**
1. Factor out the common binomial $(b + 4)$:
   $$(b + 4)(bm - 6m)$$
2. Factor out the common monomial $m$:
   $$(b + 4)(m)(b - 6) = m(b + 4)(b - 6)$$

### Exercise 4: Sum of squares
**Problem:** $a^2(a + 7) + 9(a + 7)$

**Solution:**
1. Factor out the common binomial $(a + 7)$:
   $$(a + 7)(a^2 + 9)$$

### Exercise 5: Sign manipulation
**Problem:** $3b(b - 4) - 7(4 - b)$

**Solution:**
1. Change $(4 - b)$ to $-(b - 4)$:
   $$3b(b - 4) + 7(b - 4)$$
2. Factor out the common binomial $(b - 4)$:
   $$(b - 4)(3b + 7)$$

### Exercise 6: Commutative property
**Problem:** $3g(z + 6) + 2(6 + z)$

**Solution:**
1. Note that $(6 + z)$ is the same as $(z + 6)$:
   $$3g(z + 6) + 2(z + 6)$$
2. Factor out the common binomial $(z + 6)$:
   $$(z + 6)(3g + 2)$$

### Exercise 7: Commutative property
**Problem:** $4b(y + 2) + 5(2 + y)$

**Solution:**
1. Note that $(2 + y)$ is the same as $(y + 2)$:
   $$4b(y + 2) + 5(y + 2)$$
2. Factor out the common binomial $(y + 2)$:
   $$(y + 2)(4b + 5)$$

### Exercise 8: Commutative property
**Problem:** $3d(r + 5) + 14(5 + r)$

**Solution:**
1. Note that $(5 + r)$ is the same as $(r + 5)$:
   $$3d(r + 5) + 14(r + 5)$$
2. Factor out the common binomial $(r + 5)$:
   $$(r + 5)(3d + 14)$$

---

### Factorisation: Difference of Two Squares

The following exercises demonstrate the application of the **Difference of Two Squares** formula:
$$x^2 - y^2 = (x - y)(x + y)$$

---

#### Worked Examples

**9. Factorise $(6x + y)^2 - 9$**
$$(6x + y)^2 - 9 = (6x + y - 3)(6x + y + 3)$$

**10. Factorise $4x^2 - (4x - 3y)^2$**
$$4x^2 - (4x - 3y)^2 = (2x + (4x - 3y))(2x - (4x - 3y))$$
$$= (6x - 3y)(2x - 4x + 3y)$$
$$= (6x - 3y)(3y - 2x)$$
$$= 3(2x - y)(3y - 2x)$$

**11. Factorise $16a^2 - (3b + 4c)^2$**
$$16a^2 - (3b + 4c)^2 = (4a + (3b + 4c))(4a - (3b + 4c))$$
$$= (4a + 3b + 4c)(4a - 3b - 4c)$$

**12. Factorise $(b - 4)^2 - 9(b - 5)^2$**
$$(b - 4)^2 - 9(b - 5)^2 = (b - 4 - 3(b - 5))(b - 4 + 3(b - 5))$$
$$= (b - 4 - 3b + 15)(b - 4 + 3b - 15)$$
$$= (-2b + 11)(4b - 19)$$

**13. Factorise $4(a - 3)^2 - 49(4a - 5)^2$**
$$4(a - 3)^2 - 49(4a - 5)^2 = (2(a - 3) - 7(4a - 5))(2(a - 3) + 7(4a - 5))$$
$$= (2a - 6 - 28a + 35)(2a - 6 + 28a - 35)$$
$$= (29 - 26a)(30a - 41)$$

**14. Factorise $16k^2 - 4$**
$$16k^2 - 4 = (4k - 2)(4k + 2)$$

**15. Factorise $a^2b^2c^2 - 1$**
$$a^2b^2c^2 - 1 = (abc - 1)(abc + 1)$$

**16. Factorise $\frac{1}{9}a^2 - 4b^2$**
$$\frac{1}{9}a^2 - 4b^2 = \left(\frac{1}{3}a - 2b\right)\left(\frac{1}{3}a + 2b\right)$$

**17. Factorise $\frac{1}{2}x^2 - 2$**
$$\frac{1}{2}x^2 - 2 = 2\left(\frac{1}{4}x^2 - 1\right)$$
$$= 2\left(\frac{1}{2}x + 1\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}x - 1\right)$$

---

### Factorisation: Difference of Two Squares

When factorising expressions of the form $x^2 - a$, we use the identity $x^2 - a = (x - \sqrt{a})(x + \sqrt{a})$.

**Worked Examples:**

18. Factorise $y^2 - 8$
    *   Note that $(\sqrt{8})^2 = 8$
    *   Solution: $y^2 - 8 = (y - \sqrt{8})(y + \sqrt{8})$

19. Factorise $y^2 - 13$
    *   Note that $(\sqrt{13})^2 = 13$
    *   Solution: $y^2 - 13 = (y - \sqrt{13})(y + \sqrt{13})$

20. Factorise $a^2(a - 2ab - 15b^2) - 9b^2(a^2 - 2ab - 15b^2)$
    *   Solution:
        $$a^2(a - 2ab - 15b^2) - 9b^2(a^2 - 2ab - 15b^2) = (a^2 - 2ab - 15b^2)(a^2 - 9b^2)$$
        $$= (a - 5b)(a + 3b)(a - 3b)(a + 3b)$$
        $$= (a - 3b)(a - 5b)(a + 3b)^2$$

---

### Factorising by Grouping in Pairs

This method involves grouping terms with common factors to simplify the expression.

**Exercises:**

1. Factorise $6d - 9r + 2t^5d - 3t^5r$
    *   Solution:
        $$6d - 9r + 2t^5d - 3t^5r = 3(2d - 3r) + t^5(2d - 3r)$$
        $$= (2d - 3r)(3 + t^5)$$

2. Factorise $9z - 18m + b^3z - 2b^3m$
    *   Solution:
        $$9z - 18m + b^3z - 2b^3m = 9(z - 2m) + b^3(z - 2m)$$
        $$= (z - 2m)(9 + b^3)$$

3. Factorise $35z - 10y + 7c^5z - 2c^5y$
    *   Solution:
        $$35z - 10y + 7c^5z - 2c^5y = 5(7z - 2y) + c^5(7z - 2y)$$
        $$= (7z - 2y)(5 + c^5)$$

4. Factorise $6x + a + 2ax + 3$

<!-- DIAGRAM_PLACEHOLDER: diagram -->

---

### Factorisation by Grouping

This section covers the factorisation of algebraic expressions consisting of four terms by grouping them into pairs to identify a common binomial factor.

---

#### Worked Examples

**5. Factorise: $x^2 - 6x + 5x - 30$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$x^2 - 6x + 5x - 30 = x(x - 6) + 5(x - 6)$$
    $$= (x + 5)(x - 6)$$

**6. Factorise: $5x + 10y - ax - 2ay$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$5x + 10y - ax - 2ay = 5(x + 2y) - a(x + 2y)$$
    $$= (5 - a)(x + 2y)$$

**7. Factorise: $a^2 - 2a - ax + 2x$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$a^2 - 2a - ax + 2x = a(a - 2) - x(a - 2)$$
    $$= (a - x)(a - 2)$$

**8. Factorise: $5xy - 3y + 10x - 6$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$5xy - 3y + 10x - 6 = y(5x - 3) + 2(5x - 3)$$
    $$= (y + 2)(5x - 3)$$

**9. Factorise: $ab - a^2 - a + b$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$ab - a^2 - a + b = -a^2 - a + ab + b$$
    $$= -a(a + 1) + b(a + 1)$$
    $$= (-a + b)(a + 1)$$

**10. Factorise: $14m - 4n + 7jm - 2jn$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$14m - 4n + 7jm - 2jn = 2(7m - 2n) + j(7m - 2n)$$
    $$= (7m - 2n)(2 + j)$$

**11. Factorise: $28r - 20x + 7gr - 5gx$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$28r - 20x + 7gr - 5gx = 4(7r - 5x) + g(7r - 5x)$$
    $$= (7r - 5x)(4 + g)$$

**12. Factorise: $25d - 15m + 5yd - 3ym$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$25d - 15m + 5yd - 3ym = 5(5d - 3m) + y(5d - 3m)$$
    $$= (5d - 3m)(5 + y)$$

---

### Factorisation by Grouping

Factorisation by grouping involves grouping terms in an expression that share common factors, factoring those groups, and then identifying a common binomial factor to complete the factorisation.

#### Worked Examples

**13. Factorise: $45q - 18z + 5cq - 2cz$**
*   Group terms: $(45q - 18z) + (5cq - 2cz)$
*   Factor out common terms: $9(5q - 2z) + c(5q - 2z)$
*   Final result: $(5q - 2z)(9 + c)$

**14. Factorise: $6j - 15v + 2yj - 5yv$**
*   Group terms: $(6j - 15v) + (2yj - 5yv)$
*   Factor out common terms: $3(2j - 5v) + y(2j - 5v)$
*   Final result: $(2j - 5v)(3 + y)$

**15. Factorise: $16a - 40k + 2za - 5zk$**
*   Group terms: $(16a - 40k) + (2za - 5zk)$
*   Factor out common terms: $8(2a - 5k) + z(2a - 5k)$
*   Final result: $(2a - 5k)(8 + z)$

**16. Factorise: $ax - bx + ay - by + 2a - 2b$**
*   Group terms: $(ax - bx) + (ay - by) + (2a - 2b)$
*   Factor out common terms: $x(a - b) + y(a - b) + 2(a - b)$
*   Final result: $(a - b)(x + y + 2)$

**17. Factorise: $3ax + bx - 3ay - by - 9a - 3b$**
*   Group terms: $(3ax + bx) - (3ay + by) - (9a + 3b)$
*   Factor out common terms: $x(3a + b) - y(3a + b) - 3(3a + b)$
*   Final result: $(3a + b)(x - y - 3)$

---

### Factorising a Quadratic Trinomial

A quadratic trinomial is an expression in the form $ax^2 + bx + c$. To factorise these, we look for two numbers that multiply to give the constant term ($c$) and add to give the coefficient of the middle term ($b$).

#### Worked Example

**1. Factorise: $x^2 + 8x + 15$**
*   We need two numbers that multiply to $15$ and add to $8$.
*   The factors of $15$ are $(1, 15)$ and $(3, 5)$.
*   Since $3 + 5 = 8$, we use $3$ and $5$.
*   Final result: $(x + 5)(x + 3)$

---

### 1.7 Factorisation: Worked Examples

This section provides solutions for the factorisation of quadratic trinomials.

---

**2. Factorise $x^2 + 9x + 8$**
**Solution:**
$$x^2 + 9x + 8 = (x + 8)(x + 1)$$

**3. Factorise $x^2 + 12x + 36$**
**Solution:**
$$x^2 + 12x + 36 = (x + 6)(x + 6) = (x + 6)^2$$

**4. Factorise $2h^2 + 5h - 3$**
**Solution:**
$$2h^2 + 5h - 3 = (h + 3)(2h - 1)$$

**5. Factorise $3x^2 + 4x + 1$**
**Solution:**
$$3x^2 + 4x + 1 = (x + 1)(3x + 1)$$

**6. Factorise $3s^2 + s - 10$**
**Solution:**
$$3s^2 + s - 10 = (s + 2)(3s - 5)$$

**7. Factorise $x^2 - 2x - 15$**
**Solution:**
$$x^2 - 2x - 15 = (x + 3)(x - 5)$$

**8. Factorise $x^2 + 2x - 3$**
**Solution:**
$$x^2 + 2x - 3 = (x + 3)(x - 1)$$

**9. Factorise $x^2 + x - 20$**
**Solution:**
$$x^2 + x - 20 = (x + 5)(x - 4)$$

**10. Factorise $x^2 - x - 20$**
**Solution:**
$$x^2 - x - 20 = (x - 5)(x + 4)$$

**11. Factorise $2x^2 - 22x + 20$**
**Solution:**
$$2x^2 + 22x + 20 = 2(x^2 + 11x + 10)$$
$$= 2(x + 1)(x + 10)$$

**12. Factorise $6a^2 + 14a + 8$**
**Solution:**
$$6a^2 + 14a + 8 = 2(3a^2 + 7a + 4)$$
$$= 2(a + 1)(3a + 4)$$

**13. Factorise $6v^2 - 27v + 27$**
**Solution:**
$$6v^2 - 27v + 27 = 3(2v^2 - 9v + 9)$$
$$= 3(2v - 3)(v - 3)$$

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Factorisation Exercises

This section covers the factorisation of trinomials, including those requiring a common factor first, and complex trinomials involving brackets.

---

#### 14. Factorise $6g^2 - 15g - 9$
**Solution:**
First, extract the common factor of $3$:
$$6g^2 - 15g - 9 = 3(2g^2 - 5g - 3)$$
Factorise the resulting trinomial:
$$= 3(g - 3)(2g + 1)$$

#### 15. Factorise $3x^2 + 19x + 6$
**Solution:**
$$3x^2 + 19x + 6 = (3x + 1)(x + 6)$$

#### 16. Factorise $3x^2 + 17x - 6$
**Solution:**
$$3x^2 + 17x - 6 = (3x - 1)(x + 6)$$

#### 17. Factorise $7x^2 - 6x - 1$
**Solution:**
$$7x^2 - 6x - 1 = (7x + 1)(x - 1)$$

#### 18. Factorise $6x^2 - 15x - 9$
**Solution:**
First, extract the common factor of $3$:
$$6x^2 - 15x - 9 = 3(2x^2 - 5x - 3)$$
Factorise the resulting trinomial:
$$= 3(2x + 1)(x - 3)$$

#### 19. Factorise $a^2 - 7ab + 12b^2$
**Solution:**
$$a^2 - 7ab + 12b^2 = (a - 4b)(a - 3b)$$

#### 20. Factorise $3a^2 + 5ab - 12b^2$
**Solution:**
$$3a^2 + 5ab - 12b^2 = (3a - 4b)(a + 3b)$$

#### 21. Factorise $98x^4 + 14x^2 - 4$
**Solution:**
First, extract the common factor of $2$:
$$98x^4 + 14x^2 - 4 = 2(49x^4 + 7x^2 - 2)$$
Factorise the resulting trinomial:
$$= 2(7x^2 + 2)(7x^2 - 1)$$

#### 22. Factorise $(x - 2)^2 - 7(x - 2) + 12$
**Solution:**
Treat $(x - 2)$ as a single variable:
$$(x - 2)^2 - 7(x - 2) + 12 = ((x - 2) - 4)((x - 2) - 3)$$
Simplify the terms:
$$= (x - 6)(x - 5)$$

#### 23. Factorise $(a - 2)^2 - 4(a - 2) - 5$
**Solution:**
Treat $(a - 2)$ as a single variable:
$$(a - 2)^2 - 4(a - 2) - 5 = ((a - 2) - 5)((a - 2) + 1)$$
Simplify the terms:
$$= (a - 7)(a - 1)$$

#### 24. Factorise $(y + 3)^2 - 3(y + 3) - 18$
**Solution:**
Treat $(y + 3)$ as a single variable:
$$(y + 3)^2 - 3(y + 3) - 18 = ((y + 3) - 6)((y + 3) + 3)$$
Simplify the terms:
$$= (y - 3)(y + 6)$$

---

### Factorisation of Algebraic Expressions

#### Worked Examples: Common Factor and Trinomials

**25. Factorise: $3(b^2 + 5b) + 12$**
**Solution:**
$$3(b^2 + 5b) + 12 = 3(b^2 + 5b) + 3(4)$$
$$= 3(b^2 + 5b + 4)$$
$$= 3(b + 4)(b + 1)$$

**26. Factorise: $6(a^2 + 3a) - 168$**
**Solution:**
$$6(a^2 + 3a) - 168 = 6(a^2 + 3a) - 6(28)$$
$$= 6(a^2 + 3a - 28)$$
$$= 6(a + 7)(a - 4)$$

---

### Sum and Difference of Two Cubes

The sum and difference of two cubes follow these standard factorisation patterns:
*   **Sum of cubes:** $a^3 + b^3 = (a + b)(a^2 - ab + b^2)$
*   **Difference of cubes:** $a^3 - b^3 = (a - b)(a^2 + ab + b^2)$

#### Exercise 1 – 9: Factorise the following

**1. $w^3 - 8$**
**Solution:**
$$w^3 - 8 = (w - 2)(w^2 + 2w + 4)$$

**2. $g^3 + 64$**
**Solution:**
$$g^3 + 64 = (g + 4)(g^2 - 4g + 16)$$

**3. $h^3 + 1$**
**Solution:**
$$h^3 + 1 = (h + 1)(h^2 - h + 1)$$

**4. $x^3 + 8$**
**Solution:**
$$x^3 + 8 = (x + 2)[(x)^2 - (x)(2) + (2)^2]$$
$$= (x + 2)(x^2 - 2x + 4)$$

**5. $27 - m^3$**
**Solution:**
*(Note: This follows the difference of cubes pattern $a^3 - b^3$ where $a=3$ and $b=m$)*
$$27 - m^3 = (3 - m)(3^2 + 3m + m^2)$$
$$= (3 - m)(9 + 3m + m^2)$$

### Factorisation of Algebraic Expressions

This section focuses on the factorisation of sums and differences of cubes, as well as the difference of squares.

#### Key Concepts: Factorisation Formulas
*   **Difference of Cubes:** $a^3 - b^3 = (a - b)(a^2 + ab + b^2)$
*   **Sum of Cubes:** $a^3 + b^3 = (a + b)(a^2 - ab + b^2)$
*   **Difference of Squares:** $a^2 - b^2 = (a - b)(a + b)$

---

#### Worked Examples

**6. Factorise $2x^3 - 2y^3$**
$$2x^3 - 2y^3 = 2(x^3 - y^3)$$
$$= 2(x - y)(x^2 + xy + y^2)$$

**7. Factorise $3k^3 + 81q^3$**
$$3k^3 + 81q^3 = 3(k^3 + 27q^3)$$
$$= 3(k + 3q)((k)^2 - (k)(3q) + (3q)^2)$$
$$= 3(k + 3q)(k^2 - 3kq + 9q^2)$$

**8. Factorise $64t^3 - 1$**
$$64t^3 - 1 = (4t - 1)((4t)^2 + (4t)(1) + (1)^2)$$
$$= (4t - 1)(16t^2 + 4t + 1)$$

**9. Factorise $64x^2 - 1$**
$$64x^2 - 1 = (8x - 1)(8x + 1)$$

**10. Factorise $125x^3 + 1$**
$$125x^3 + 1 = (5x + 1)((5x)^2 - (5x)(1) + (1)^2)$$
$$= (5x + 1)(25x^2 - 5x + 1)$$

**11. Factorise $25x^3 + 1$**
*Note: $(\sqrt[3]{25})^3 = 25$*
$$25x^3 + 1 = (\sqrt[3]{25}x + 1)((\sqrt[3]{25}x)^2 - (\sqrt[3]{25}x)(1) + (1)^2)$$
$$= (\sqrt[3]{25}x + 1)((\sqrt[3]{25})^2x^2 - \sqrt[3]{25}x + 1)$$

**12. Factorise $z - 125z^4$**
$$z - 125z^4 = z(1 - 125z^3)$$
$$= z(1 - 5z)((1)^2 + (1)(5z) + (5z)^2)$$
$$= z(1 - 5z)(1 + 5z + 25z^2)$$

---

### Factorisation: Sum and Difference of Two Cubes

The following exercises demonstrate the factorisation of algebraic expressions using the sum and difference of cubes formulas:
*   **Sum of two cubes:** $a^3 + b^3 = (a + b)(a^2 - ab + b^2)$
*   **Difference of two cubes:** $a^3 - b^3 = (a - b)(a^2 + ab + b^2)$

---

#### Worked Examples

**14. Factorise $216n^3 - k^3$**
$$216n^3 - k^3 = (6n - k)(36n^2 + 6nk + k^2)$$

**15. Factorise $125s^3 + d^3$**
$$125s^3 + d^3 = (5s + d)(25s^2 - 5sd + d^2)$$

**16. Factorise $8k^3 + r^3$**
$$8k^3 + r^3 = (2k + r)(4k^2 - 2kr + r^2)$$

**17. Factorise $8j^3k^3l^3 - b^3$**
$$8j^3k^3l^3 - b^3 = (2jkl - b)(4j^2k^2l^2 + 2jklb + b^2)$$

**18. Factorise $27x^3y^3 + w^3$**
$$27x^3y^3 + w^3 = (3xy + w)(9x^2y^2 - 3xyw + w^2)$$

**19. Factorise $128m^3 + 2f^3$**
First, extract the common factor of $2$:
$$128m^3 + 2f^3 = 2(64m^3 + f^3)$$
Then, factorise the sum of cubes:
$$= 2(4m + f)(16m^2 - 4mf + f^2)$$

**20. Factorise $p^{15} - \frac{1}{8}y^{12}$**
$$p^{15} - \frac{1}{8}y^{12} = (p^5)^3 - (\frac{1}{2}y^4)^3$$
$$= (p^5 - \frac{1}{2}y^4)[(p^5)^2 + (p^5)(\frac{1}{2}y^4) + (\frac{1}{2}y^4)^2]$$
$$= (p^5 - \frac{1}{2}y^4)(p^{10} + \frac{1}{2}p^5y^4 + \frac{1}{4}y^8)$$

**21. Factorise $\frac{27}{t^3} - s^3$**
$$\frac{27}{t^3} - s^3 = (\frac{3}{t} - s)(\frac{9}{t^2} + \frac{3s}{t} + s^2)$$

**22. Factorise $\frac{1}{64q^3} - h^3$**
$$\frac{1}{64q^3} - h^3 = (\frac{1}{4q} - h)(\frac{1}{16q^2} + \frac{h}{4q} + h^2)$$

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Factorisation and Simplification

#### Worked Examples: Factorisation by Grouping and Special Products

**23. Factorise: $72g^3 + \frac{1}{3}v^3$**
**Solution:**
$$72g^3 + \frac{1}{3}v^3 = \frac{1}{3}(216g^3 + v^3)$$
Using the sum of cubes formula $a^3 + b^3 = (a+b)(a^2 - ab + b^2)$:
$$= \frac{1}{3}(6g + v)(36g^2 - 6gv + v^2)$$

**24. Factorise: $1 - (x - y)^3$**
**Solution:**
Using the difference of cubes formula $a^3 - b^3 = (a-b)(a^2 + ab + b^2)$:
$$1 - (x - y)^3 = (1 - (x - y))((1)^2 + (1)(x - y) + (x - y)^2)$$
$$= (1 - x + y)(1 - x + y + x^2 - 2xy + y^2)$$

**25. Factorise: $h^4(8g^6 + h^3) - (8g^6 + h^3)$**
**Solution:**
Factor out the common binomial $(8g^6 + h^3)$:
$$h^4(8g^6 + h^3) - (8g^6 + h^3) = (h^4 - 1)(8g^6 + h^3)$$
Factor using difference of squares and sum of cubes:
$$= (h^2 - 1)(h^2 + 1)(2g^2 + h)(4g^4 - 2g^2h + h^2)$$
$$= (h - 1)(h + 1)(h^2 + 1)(2g^2 + h)(4g^4 - 2g^2h + h^2)$$

**26. Factorise: $x(125w^3 - h^3) + y(125w^3 - h^3)$**
**Solution:**
Factor out the common binomial $(125w^3 - h^3)$:
$$x(125w^3 - h^3) + y(125w^3 - h^3) = (x + y)(125w^3 - h^3)$$
Using the difference of cubes formula:
$$= (x + y)(5w - h)(25w^2 + 5wh + h^2)$$

**27. Factorise: $x^2(27p^3 + w^3) - 5x(27p^3 + w^3) - 6(27p^3 + w^3)$**
**Solution:**
Factor out the common binomial $(27p^3 + w^3)$:
$$x^2(27p^3 + w^3) - 5x(27p^3 + w^3) - 6(27p^3 + w^3) = (x^2 - 5x - 6)(27p^3 + w^3)$$
Factor the quadratic and the sum of cubes:
$$= (x - 6)(x + 1)(3p + w)(9p^2 - 3pw + w^2)$$

---

### 1.8 Simplification of Fractions

**Concept:** To simplify algebraic fractions, factorise the numerator and the denominator completely, then cancel out common factors.

**Exercise 1:**
Simplify the following expression (assume all denominators are non-zero):

**a)** $\frac{3a}{15}$

**Solution:**
Divide both the numerator and denominator by the common factor $3$:
$$\frac{3a}{15} = \frac{a}{5}$$

<!-- DIAGRAM_PLACEHOLDER: diagram -->

---

### 1.8 Simplification of Algebraic Fractions

To simplify algebraic fractions, we factorize the numerator and the denominator where possible, then cancel out common factors.

#### Worked Examples

**Example a)**
$$\frac{3a}{15} = \frac{a}{5}$$

**Example b)**
Simplify: $\frac{2a + 10}{4}$
**Solution:**
$$\frac{2a + 10}{4} = \frac{2(a + 5)}{4}$$
$$= \frac{a + 5}{2}$$

**Example c)**
Simplify: $\frac{5a + 20}{a + 4}$
**Solution:**
$$\frac{5a + 20}{a + 4} = \frac{5(a + 4)}{a + 4}$$
$$= 5$$

**Example d)**
Simplify: $\frac{a^2 - 4a}{a - 4}$
**Solution:**
$$\frac{a^2 - 4a}{a - 4} = \frac{a(a - 4)}{a - 4}$$
$$= a$$

**Example e)**
Simplify: $\frac{3a^2 - 9a}{2a - 6}$
**Solution:**
$$\frac{3a^2 - 9a}{2a - 6} = \frac{3a(a - 3)}{2(a - 3)}$$
$$= \frac{3a}{2}$$

**Example f)**
Simplify: $\frac{9a + 27}{9a + 18}$
**Solution:**
$$\frac{9a + 27}{9a + 18} = \frac{9(a + 3)}{9(a + 2)}$$
$$= \frac{a + 3}{a + 2}$$
*Note restriction: $a \neq -2$*

**Example g)**
Simplify: $\frac{6ab + 2a}{2b}$
**Solution:**
$$\frac{6ab + 2a}{2b} = \frac{2a(3b + 1)}{2b}$$
$$= \frac{a(3b + 1)}{b}$$
*Note restriction: $b \neq 0$*

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Simplifying Rational Expressions

This section focuses on simplifying algebraic fractions by factoring both the numerator and the denominator, then canceling common factors.

---

#### h) Simplify: $\frac{16x^2y - 8xy}{12x - 6}$

**Solution:**
Factor out the common terms in the numerator and denominator:
$$\frac{16x^2y - 8xy}{12x - 6} = \frac{8xy(2x - 1)}{6(2x - 1)}$$
Cancel the common factor $(2x - 1)$:
$$= \frac{8xy}{6}$$
Simplify the fraction:
$$= \frac{4xy}{3}$$

---

#### i) Simplify: $\frac{4xyp - 8xp}{12xy}$

**Solution:**
Factor out the common terms in the numerator:
$$\frac{4xyp - 8xp}{12xy} = \frac{4xp(y - 2)}{12xy}$$
Cancel the common factor $4x$:
$$= \frac{p(y - 2)}{3y}$$
*Note restriction: $y \neq 0$.*

---

#### j) Simplify: $\frac{9x^2 - 16}{6x - 8}$

**Solution:**
Factor the numerator (difference of two squares) and the denominator:
$$\frac{9x^2 - 16}{6x - 8} = \frac{(3x - 4)(3x + 4)}{2(3x - 4)}$$
Cancel the common factor $(3x - 4)$:
$$= \frac{3x + 4}{2}$$

---

#### k) Simplify: $\frac{b^2 - 81a^2}{18a - 2b}$

**Solution:**
Factor the numerator (difference of two squares) and the denominator:
$$\frac{b^2 - 81a^2}{18a - 2b} = \frac{(b - 9a)(b + 9a)}{2(9a - b)}$$
*Note: $(b - 9a) = -(9a - b)$.*
$$= \frac{-(9a - b)(b + 9a)}{2(9a - b)}$$
Cancel the common factor $(9a - b)$:
$$= -\frac{b + 9a}{2}$$

---

#### l) Simplify: $\frac{t^2 - s^2}{s^2 - 2st + t^2}$

**Solution:**
Factor the numerator (difference of two squares) and the denominator (perfect square trinomial):
$$\frac{t^2 - s^2}{s^2 - 2st + t^2} = \frac{(t - s)(t + s)}{(s - t)^2}$$
*Note: $(t - s) = -(s - t)$.*
$$= \frac{-(s - t)(t + s)}{(s - t)^2}$$
Cancel the common factor $(s - t)$:
$$= \frac{-(t + s)}{s - t}$$

---

#### m) Simplify: $\frac{x^2 - 2x - 15}{5x - 25}$

**Solution:**
Factor the numerator (trinomial) and the denominator:
$$\frac{x^2 - 2x - 15}{5x - 25} = \frac{(x - 5)(x + 3)}{5(x - 5)}$$
Cancel the common factor $(x - 5)$:
$$= \frac{x + 3}{5}$$
*Note restriction: $s \neq t$ (from previous problem context).*

---

### Simplification of Algebraic Fractions

To simplify algebraic fractions, factorize both the numerator and the denominator completely, then cancel out common factors. Always note the restrictions where the denominator equals zero, as division by zero is undefined.

---

#### Worked Examples

**n) Simplify:** $\frac{x^2 + 2x - 15}{x^2 + 8x + 15}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{x^2 + 2x - 15}{x^2 + 8x + 15} = \frac{(x + 5)(x - 3)}{(x + 3)(x + 5)}$$
$$= \frac{x - 3}{x + 3}$$
*Restriction: $x \neq -3$*

---

**o) Simplify:** $\frac{x^2 - x - 6}{x^3 - 27}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{x^2 - x - 6}{x^3 - 27} = \frac{(x - 3)(x + 2)}{(x - 3)(x^2 + 3x + 9)}$$
$$= \frac{x + 2}{x^2 + 3x + 9}$$

---

**p) Simplify:** $\frac{a^2 + 6a - 16}{a^3 - 8}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{a^2 + 6a - 16}{a^3 - 8} = \frac{(a + 8)(a - 2)}{(a - 2)(a^2 + 2a + 4)}$$
$$= \frac{a + 8}{a^2 + 2a + 4}$$

---

**q) Simplify:** $\frac{a^2 - 4ab - 12b^2}{a^2 + 4ab + 4b^2}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{a^2 - 4ab - 12b^2}{a^2 + 4ab + 4b^2} = \frac{(a - 6b)(a + 2b)}{(a + 2b)^2}$$
$$= \frac{a - 6b}{a + 2b}$$
*Restriction: $a \neq -2b$*

---

**r) Simplify:** $\frac{6a^2 - 7a - 3}{3ab + b}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{6a^2 - 7a - 3}{3ab + b} = \frac{(2a - 3)(3a + 1)}{b(3a + 1)}$$
$$= \frac{2a - 3}{b}$$
*Restriction: $b \neq 0$*

---

**s) Simplify:** $\frac{2x^2 - x - 1}{x^3 - x}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{2x^2 - x - 1}{x^3 - x} = \frac{(2x + 1)(x - 1)}{x(x - 1)(x + 1)}$$
$$= \frac{2x + 1}{x(x + 1)}$$
*Restrictions: $x \neq -1$ and $x \neq 0$*

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Factorization and Simplification

This section focuses on simplifying algebraic fractions using grouping and factorization techniques.

---

#### 1. Simplifying Fractions by Grouping
To simplify expressions with four terms in the numerator, group the terms to factor out common binomials.

**t) Simplify:** $\frac{qz + qr + 16z + 16r}{z + r}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{qz + qr + 16z + 16r}{(z + r)} = \frac{q(z + r) + 16(z + r)}{(z + r)}$$
$$= \frac{(z + r)(q + 16)}{(z + r)}$$
$$= q + 16$$

**u) Simplify:** $\frac{pz - pq + 5z - 5q}{z - q}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{pz - pq + 5z - 5q}{(z - q)} = \frac{p(z - q) + 5(z - q)}{(z - q)}$$
$$= \frac{(z - q)(p + 5)}{(z - q)}$$
$$= p + 5$$

**v) Simplify:** $\frac{hx - hg + 13x - 13g}{x - g}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{hx - hg + 13x - 13g}{(x - g)} = \frac{h(x - g) + 13(x - g)}{(x - g)}$$
$$= \frac{(x - g)(h + 13)}{(x - g)}$$
$$= h + 13$$

**w) Simplify:** $\frac{f^2a - fa^2}{f - a}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{f^2a - fa^2}{f - a} = \frac{af(f - a)}{(f - a)}$$
$$= af$$

---

#### 2. Division of Algebraic Fractions
When dividing algebraic fractions, multiply by the reciprocal of the divisor. Ensure all denominators are non-zero.

**a) Simplify:** $\frac{b^2 + 10b + 21}{3(b^2 - 9)} \div \frac{2b^2 + 14b}{30b^2 - 90b}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{b^2 + 10b + 21}{3(b^2 - 9)} \times \frac{30b^2 - 90b}{2b^2 + 14b}$$
$$= \frac{(b + 7)(b + 3)}{3(b - 3)(b + 3)} \times \frac{30b(b - 3)}{2b(b + 7)}$$
$$= \frac{1}{3} \times \frac{30}{2}$$
$$= 5$$

**b) Simplify:** $\frac{x^2 + 17x + 70}{5(x^2 - 100)} \div \frac{3x^2 + 21x}{45x^2 - 450x}$

*(Note: This problem is presented as an exercise for the learner to apply the steps demonstrated in part (a).)*

---

### Algebraic Fractions: Multiplication and Division

To simplify algebraic fractions involving multiplication and division, follow these steps:
1. **Factorise** all numerators and denominators completely.
2. For **division**, multiply by the reciprocal of the divisor (flip the second fraction).
3. **Cancel** common factors between the numerators and denominators.
4. **Simplify** the remaining expression.

---

#### Worked Examples

**c) Simplify:** $\frac{z^2 + 17z + 66}{3(z^2 - 121)} \div \frac{2z^2 + 12z}{24z^2 - 264z}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{z^2 + 17z + 66}{3(z^2 - 121)} \div \frac{2z^2 + 12z}{24z^2 - 264z} = \frac{z^2 + 17z + 66}{3(z^2 - 121)} \times \frac{24z^2 - 264z}{2z^2 + 12z}$$
$$= \frac{(z + 6)(z + 11)}{3(z - 11)(z + 11)} \times \frac{24z(z - 11)}{2z(z + 6)}$$
$$= \frac{1}{3} \times \frac{24}{2}$$
$$= 4$$

---

**d) Simplify:** $\frac{3a + 9}{14} \div \frac{7a + 21}{a + 3}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{3a + 9}{14} \div \frac{7a + 21}{a + 3} = \frac{3(a + 3)}{14} \div \frac{7(a + 3)}{a + 3}$$
$$= \frac{3(a + 3)}{14} \div 7$$
$$= \frac{3(a + 3)}{14} \times \frac{1}{7}$$
$$= \frac{3(a + 3)}{98}$$

---

**e) Simplify:** $\frac{a^2 - 5a}{2a + 10} \times \frac{4a}{3a + 15}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{a^2 - 5a}{2a + 10} \times \frac{4a}{3a + 15} = \frac{a(a - 5)}{2(a + 5)} \times \frac{4a}{3(a + 5)}$$
$$= \frac{[a(a - 5)][4a]}{[2(a + 5)][3(a + 5)]}$$
$$= \frac{4a^2(a - 5)}{6(a + 5)^2}$$
*(Note: This can be further simplified to $\frac{2a^2(a - 5)}{3(a + 5)^2}$)*

---

**f) Simplify:** $\frac{3xp + 4p}{8p} \div \frac{12p^2}{3x + 4}$
*Note restriction: $a \neq -5$*

**Solution:**
$$\frac{3xp + 4p}{8p} \div \frac{12p^2}{3x + 4} = \frac{p(3x + 4)}{8p} \div \frac{12p^2}{3x + 4}$$
$$= \frac{3x + 4}{8} \times \frac{3x + 4}{12p^2}$$
$$= \frac{(3x + 4)[3x + 4]}{[8][12p^2]}$$
$$= \frac{(3x + 4)^2}{96p^2}$$

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Multiplication and Division of Fractions

To simplify algebraic fractions involving multiplication and division, follow these steps:
1. **Factorise** all numerators and denominators completely.
2. **Invert** the divisor fraction when dividing (multiply by the reciprocal).
3. **Cancel** common factors between the numerators and denominators.
4. **Simplify** the remaining expression.

---

#### Worked Example g)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{24a - 8}{12} \div \frac{9a - 3}{6}$ (Restriction: $p \neq 0$)

**Solution:**
$$\frac{24a - 8}{12} \div \frac{9a - 3}{6} = \frac{8(3a - 1)}{12} \div \frac{3(a - 1)}{6}$$
$$= \frac{2(3a - 1)}{3} \times \frac{2}{a - 1}$$
$$= \frac{[2(3a - 1)][2]}{[3][a - 1]}$$
$$= \frac{4(3a - 1)}{3(a - 1)}$$

---

#### Worked Example h)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{a^2 + 2a}{5} \div \frac{2a + 4}{20}$ (Restriction: $a \neq 1$)

**Solution:**
$$\frac{a^2 + 2a}{5} \div \frac{2a + 4}{20} = \frac{a(a + 2)}{5} \div \frac{2(a + 2)}{20}$$
$$= \frac{a(a + 2)}{5} \times \frac{10}{a + 2}$$
$$= \frac{[a(a + 2)][10]}{[5][a + 2]}$$
$$= \frac{10a}{5}$$
$$= 2a$$

---

#### Worked Example i)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{p^2 + pq}{7p} \times \frac{21q}{8p + 8q}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{p^2 + pq}{7p} \times \frac{21q}{8p + 8q} = \frac{p(p + q)}{7p} \times \frac{21q}{8(p + q)}$$
$$= \frac{[p(p + q)][21q]}{[7p][8(p + q)]}$$
$$= \frac{21pq}{56p}$$
$$= \frac{3q}{8}$$

---

#### Worked Example j)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{5ab - 15b}{4a - 12} \div \frac{6b^2}{a + b}$ (Restriction: $a \neq -b$)

**Solution:**
$$\frac{5ab - 15b}{4a - 12} \div \frac{6b^2}{a + b} = \frac{5b(a - 3)}{4(a - 3)} \div \frac{6b^2}{a + b}$$
$$= \frac{5b}{4} \times \frac{a + b}{6b^2}$$
$$= \frac{[5b][a + b]}{[4][6b^2]}$$
$$= \frac{30b^3}{4(a + b)}$$

---

### Algebraic Fractions: Multiplication and Division

This section covers the simplification of complex algebraic expressions involving multiplication, division, and subtraction of fractions.

---

#### Worked Examples

**k) Simplify:** $\frac{16 - x^2}{x^2 - x - 12} \times \frac{x + 3}{x + 4}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{16 - x^2}{x^2 - x - 12} \times \frac{x + 3}{x + 4} = \frac{(4 - x)(4 + x)}{(x - 4)(x + 3)} \times \frac{x + 3}{x + 4}$$
$$= -1$$

---

**l) Simplify:** $\frac{a^3 + b^3}{a^3} \times \frac{5a + 5b}{a^2 + 2ab + b^2}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{a^3 + b^3}{a^3} \times \frac{5a + 5b}{a^2 + 2ab + b^2} = \frac{(a + b)(a^2 - ab + b^2)}{a^3} \times \frac{5(a + b)}{(a + b)^2}$$
$$= \frac{a^2 - ab + b^2}{a^3} \times 5$$
$$= \frac{5(a^2 - ab + b^2)}{a^3}$$
*Note restrictions: $a \neq 0$.*

---

**m) Simplify:** $\frac{a - 4}{a + 5a + 4} \times \frac{a^2 + 2a + 1}{a^2 - 3a - 4}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{a - 4}{a + 5a + 4} \times \frac{a^2 + 2a + 1}{a^2 - 3a - 4} = \frac{a - 4}{(a + 4)(a + 1)} \times \frac{(a + 1)^2}{(a - 4)(a + 1)}$$
$$= \frac{1}{a + 4}$$
*Note restrictions: $a \neq -4$.*

---

**n) Simplify:** $\frac{3x + 2}{x^2 - 6x + 8} \times \frac{x - 2}{3x^2 + 8x + 4}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{3x + 2}{x^2 - 6x + 8} \times \frac{x - 2}{3x^2 + 8x + 4} = \frac{3x + 2}{(x - 4)(x - 2)} \times \frac{x - 2}{(3x + 2)(x + 2)}$$
$$= \frac{1}{(x - 4)(x + 2)}$$
*Note restrictions: $x \neq 4$ and $x \neq -2$.*

---

**o) Simplify:** $\frac{a^2 - 2a + 8}{a^2 + 6a + 8} \times \frac{a^2 + a - 12}{3} - \frac{3}{2}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{a^2 - 2a + 8}{a^2 + 6a + 8} \times \frac{a^2 + a - 12}{3} - \frac{3}{2} = \frac{(a - 4)(a + 2)}{(a + 2)(a + 4)} \times \frac{(a + 4)(a - 3)}{3} - \frac{3}{2}$$
$$= \frac{(a - 4)(a - 3)}{3} - \frac{3}{2}$$
$$= \frac{2(a - 4)(a - 3) - 9}{6}$$
$$= \frac{2(a^2 - 7a + 12) - 9}{6}$$
$$= \frac{2a^2 - 14a + 15}{6}$$

---

**p) Simplify:** $\frac{4x^2 - 1}{3x^2 + 10x + 3} \div \frac{6x^2 + 5x + 1}{4x^2 + 7x - 3} \times \frac{9x^2 + 6x + 1}{8x^2 - 6x + 1}$

<!-- DIAGRAM_PLACEHOLDER: diagram -->

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Operations with Fractions

#### Worked Example: Division and Multiplication of Rational Expressions
Simplify:
$$\frac{4x^2 - 1}{3x^2 + 10x + 3} \div \frac{6x^2 + 5x + 1}{4x^2 + 7x - 3} \times \frac{9x^2 + 6x + 1}{8x^2 - 6x + 1}$$

**Solution:**
$$= \frac{(2x - 1)(2x + 1)}{(x + 3)(3x + 1)} \times \frac{(x + 3)(4x - 1)}{(2x + 1)(3x + 1)} \times \frac{(3x + 1)^2}{(2x - 1)(4x - 1)}$$
$$= 1$$

---

#### Exercise q) Subtraction of Fractions
Simplify: $\frac{x + 4}{3} - \frac{x - 2}{2}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{x + 4}{3} - \frac{x - 2}{2} = \frac{2(x + 4) - 3(x - 2)}{6}$$
$$= \frac{2x + 8 - 3x + 6}{6}$$
$$= \frac{14 - x}{6}$$

---

#### Exercise r) Multiplication of Rational Expressions
Simplify: $\frac{p^3 + q^3}{p^2} \times \frac{3p - 3q}{p^2 - q^2}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{p^3 + q^3}{p^2} \times \frac{3p - 3q}{p^2 - q^2} = \frac{(p + q)(p^2 - pq + q^2)}{p^2} \times \frac{3(p - q)}{(p - q)(p + q)}$$
$$= \frac{(p + q)(p^2 - pq + q^2)}{p^2} \times \frac{3}{p + q}$$
$$= \frac{3(p^2 - pq + q^2)}{p^2}$$
*Note restriction: $p \neq 0$.*

---

### 3. Simplify (Assume all denominators are non-zero)

#### a) $\frac{x - 3}{3} - \frac{x + 5}{4}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{x - 3}{3} - \frac{x + 5}{4} = \frac{4(x - 3) - 3(x + 5)}{12}$$
$$= \frac{4x - 12 - 3x - 15}{12}$$
$$= \frac{x - 27}{12}$$

#### b) $\frac{2x - 4}{9} - \frac{x - 3}{4} + 1$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{2x - 4}{9} - \frac{x - 3}{4} + 1 = \frac{4(2x - 4) - 9(x - 3) + 36}{36}$$
$$= \frac{8x - 16 - 9x + 27 + 36}{36}$$
$$= \frac{47 - x}{36}$$

#### c) $1 + \frac{3x - 4}{4} - \frac{x + 2}{3}$

**Solution:**

### Algebraic Fractions: Addition and Subtraction

To add or subtract algebraic fractions, you must find a **Lowest Common Denominator (LCD)**. Once the denominators are the same, you can combine the numerators while keeping the denominator unchanged. Always remember to state the restrictions for the variables, as the denominator cannot be equal to zero.

---

#### Worked Example: Introductory Concept
$$1 + \frac{3x - 4}{4} - \frac{x + 2}{3} = \frac{12 + 3(3x - 4) - 4(x + 2)}{12}$$
$$= \frac{12 + 9x - 12 - 4x - 8}{12}$$
$$= \frac{5x - 8}{12}$$

---

#### Exercise d)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{11}{a + 11} + \frac{8}{a - 8}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{11}{a + 11} + \frac{8}{a - 8} = \frac{11(a - 8) + 8(a + 11)}{(a + 11)(a - 8)}$$
$$= \frac{11a - 88 + 8a + 88}{(a + 11)(a - 8)}$$
$$= \frac{19a}{(a + 11)(a - 8)}$$

**Restrictions:** $a \neq -11$ and $a \neq 8$.

---

#### Exercise e)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{12}{x - 12} - \frac{6}{x - 6}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{12}{x - 12} - \frac{6}{x - 6} = \frac{12(x - 6) - 6(x - 12)}{(x - 12)(x - 6)}$$
$$= \frac{12x - 72 - 6x + 72}{(x - 12)(x - 6)}$$
$$= \frac{6x}{(x - 12)(x - 6)}$$

**Restrictions:** $x \neq 12$ and $x \neq 6$.

---

#### Exercise f)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{12}{r + 12} + \frac{8}{r - 8}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{12}{r + 12} + \frac{8}{r - 8} = \frac{12(r - 8) + 8(r + 12)}{(r + 12)(r - 8)}$$
$$= \frac{12r - 96 + 8r + 96}{(r + 12)(r - 8)}$$
$$= \frac{20r}{(r + 12)(r - 8)}$$

**Restrictions:** $r \neq -12$ and $r \neq 8$.

---

#### Exercise g)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{2}{xy} + \frac{4}{xz} + \frac{3}{yz}$

**Solution:**
$$\frac{2}{xy} + \frac{4}{xz} + \frac{3}{yz} = \frac{2z}{xyz} + \frac{4y}{xyz} + \frac{3x}{xyz}$$
$$= \frac{2z + 4y + 3x}{xyz}$$

**Restrictions:** $x \neq 0$; $y \neq 0$ and $z \neq 0$.

---

#### Exercise h)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{5}{t - 2} - \frac{1}{t - 3}$

*(Note: This problem is presented as an exercise for the learner to complete.)*

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Addition and Subtraction of Fractions

To add or subtract algebraic fractions, you must find a **Lowest Common Denominator (LCD)**. Once the denominators are the same, you can combine the numerators while keeping the denominator constant. Always remember to state the restrictions for the variables, as the denominator cannot be equal to zero.

---

#### Worked Example 1
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{5}{t - 2} - \frac{1}{t - 3}$

**Solution:**
1. Find the LCD: $(t - 2)(t - 3)$
2. Adjust the numerators:
$$\frac{5}{t - 2} - \frac{1}{t - 3} = \frac{5(t - 3)}{(t - 3)(t - 2)} - \frac{1(t - 2)}{(t - 2)(t - 3)}$$
3. Combine:
$$= \frac{5(t - 3) - (t - 2)}{(t - 2)(t - 3)}$$
$$= \frac{5t - 15 - t + 2}{(t - 2)(t - 3)}$$
$$= \frac{4t - 12}{(t - 2)(t - 3)}$$

**Note:** Restrictions: $t \neq 2$ and $t \neq 3$.

---

#### Worked Example 2
**Problem:** Simplify $i) \frac{k + 2}{k^2 + 2} - \frac{1}{k + 2}$

**Solution:**
1. Find the LCD: $(k^2 + 2)(k + 2)$
2. Adjust the numerators:
$$\frac{k + 2}{k^2 + 2} - \frac{1}{k + 2} = \frac{(k + 2)(k + 2)}{(k^2 + 2)(k + 2)} - \frac{1(k^2 + 2)}{(k^2 + 2)(k + 2)}$$
3. Expand and simplify:
$$= \frac{(k + 2)^2 - (k^2 + 2)}{(k^2 + 2)(k + 2)}$$
$$= \frac{k^2 + 4k + 4 - k^2 - 2}{(k^2 + 2)(k + 2)}$$
$$= \frac{4k + 2}{(k^2 + 2)(k + 2)}$$
$$= \frac{2(2k + 1)}{(k^2 + 2)(k + 2)}$$

**Note:** Restrictions: $k \neq -2$ and $k^2 \neq -2$ (or $k \neq \pm\sqrt{-2}$, which is undefined in real numbers).

---

#### Worked Example 3
**Problem:** Simplify $j) \frac{t + 2}{3q} + \frac{t + 1}{2q}$

**Solution:**
1. Find the LCD: $6q$
2. Adjust the numerators:
$$\frac{t + 2}{3q} + \frac{t + 1}{2q} = \frac{(t + 2)(2q)}{(3q)(2q)} + \frac{(t + 1)(3q)}{(3q)(2q)}$$
3. Simplify:
$$= \frac{2tq + 4q + 3tq + 3q}{6q^2}$$
$$= \frac{5tq + 7q}{6q^2}$$
$$= \frac{q(5t + 7)}{6q^2}$$
$$= \frac{5t + 7}{6q}$$

**Note:** Restriction: $q \neq 0$.

---

#### Worked Example 4
**Problem:** Simplify $k) \frac{3}{p^2 - 4} + \frac{2}{(p - 2)^2}$

**Solution:**
1. Factor the denominators: $p^2 - 4 = (p - 2)(p + 2)$.
2. Find the LCD: $(p + 2)(p - 2)^2$.
3. Adjust the numerators:
$$\frac{3}{(p - 2)(p + 2)} + \frac{2}{(p - 2)^2} = \frac{3(p - 2)}{(p + 2)(p - 2)^2} + \frac{2(p + 2)}{(p + 2)(p - 2)^2}$$
4. Simplify:
$$= \frac{3p - 6 + 2p + 4}{(p + 2)(p - 2)^2}$$
$$= \frac{5p - 2}{(p + 2)(p - 2)^2}$$

**Note:** Restriction: $p \neq \pm 2$.

---

### Algebraic Fractions: Simplification and Operations

This section covers the simplification of complex algebraic expressions involving addition, subtraction, and multiplication of fractions.

---

#### Worked Example l)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{x}{x + y} + \frac{x^2}{y^2 - x^2}$

**Solution:**
1. Factor the denominator: $y^2 - x^2 = (y - x)(y + x) = -(x - y)(x + y)$
2. Rewrite the expression:
$$\frac{x}{x + y} + \frac{x^2}{(y - x)(y + x)}$$
3. Find a common denominator: $(x + y)(x - y)$
$$\frac{x(x - y) + x^2}{(x + y)(x - y)}$$
4. Expand and simplify the numerator:
$$\frac{x^2 - xy + x^2}{(x + y)(x - y)} = \frac{2x^2 - xy}{(x + y)(x - y)}$$
*Note: Restriction $x \neq \pm y$*

---

#### Worked Example m)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{1}{m + n} + \frac{3mn}{m^3 + n^3}$

**Solution:**
1. Factor the sum of cubes: $m^3 + n^3 = (m + n)(m^2 - mn + n^2)$
2. Find the common denominator: $(m + n)(m^2 - mn + n^2)$
$$\frac{1(m^2 - mn + n^2) + 3mn}{(m + n)(m^2 - mn + n^2)}$$
3. Simplify the numerator:
$$\frac{m^2 + 2mn + n^2}{(m + n)(m^2 - mn + n^2)}$$
4. Factor the numerator: $m^2 + 2mn + n^2 = (m + n)^2$
$$\frac{(m + n)^2}{(m + n)(m^2 - mn + n^2)} = \frac{m + n}{m^2 - mn + n^2}$$

---

#### Worked Example n)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{h}{h^3 - f^3} - \frac{1}{h^2 + hf + f^2}$

**Solution:**
1. Factor the difference of cubes: $h^3 - f^3 = (h - f)(h^2 + hf + f^2)$
2. Use the common denominator $(h - f)(h^2 + hf + f^2)$:
$$\frac{h - 1(h - f)}{(h - f)(h^2 + hf + f^2)}$$
3. Simplify the numerator: $h - h + f = f$
$$\frac{f}{(h - f)(h^2 + hf + f^2)}$$

---

#### Worked Example o)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{x^2 - 1}{3} \times \frac{1}{x - 1} - \frac{1}{2}$

**Solution:**
1. Perform the multiplication first:
$$\frac{(x^2 - 1)(1)}{3(x - 1)} - \frac{1}{2} = \frac{(x - 1)(x + 1)}{3(x - 1)} - \frac{1}{2}$$
2. Cancel common terms:
$$\frac{x + 1}{3} - \frac{1}{2}$$
3. Find a common denominator (6):
$$\frac{2(x + 1) - 3(1)}{6} = \frac{2x + 2 - 3}{6} = \frac{2x - 1}{6}$$

---

#### Exercise p)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{x^2 - 2x + 1}{(x - 1)^3} - \frac{x^2 + x + 1}{x^3 - 1}$

### Algebraic Expressions: Simplifying Rational Expressions

This section covers the simplification of complex algebraic fractions involving addition and subtraction. The process requires finding a common denominator, factoring polynomials (difference of cubes, trinomials, and difference of squares), and simplifying the resulting numerators.

---

#### Worked Example: q)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{1}{(x-1)^2} - \frac{2x}{x^3-1}$

**Solution:**
1. Factor the denominator $x^3-1$ as $(x-1)(x^2+x+1)$.
2. Identify the lowest common denominator (LCD): $(x-1)^2(x^2+x+1)$.
3. Adjust the fractions:
$$\frac{1}{(x-1)^2} - \frac{2x}{x^3-1} = \frac{1}{(x-1)^2} - \frac{2x}{(x-1)(x^2+x+1)}$$
$$= \frac{x^2+x+1 - 2x(x-1)}{(x-1)^2(x^2+x+1)}$$
$$= \frac{x^2+x+1 - 2x^2+2x}{(x-1)^2(x^2+x+1)}$$
$$= \frac{-x^2+3x+1}{(x-1)^2(x^2+x+1)}$$

---

#### Worked Example: r)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{t^2+2t-8}{t^2+t-6} + \frac{1}{t^2-9} + \frac{t+1}{t-3}$

**Note:** Restriction $t \neq \pm 3$.

**Solution:**
1. Factor all denominators:
   - $t^2+t-6 = (t+3)(t-2)$
   - $t^2-9 = (t-3)(t+3)$
2. Simplify the first term: $\frac{(t+4)(t-2)}{(t+3)(t-2)} = \frac{t+4}{t+3}$
3. Combine the fractions:
$$\frac{t+4}{t+3} + \frac{1}{(t-3)(t+3)} + \frac{t+1}{t-3}$$
$$= \frac{(t-3)(t+4) + 1 + (t+1)(t+3)}{(t-3)(t+3)}$$
$$= \frac{t^2+t-12 + 1 + t^2+4t+3}{(t-3)(t+3)}$$
$$= \frac{2t^2+5t-8}{(t-3)(t+3)} = \frac{2t^2+5t-8}{t^2-9}$$

---

#### Worked Example: s)
**Problem:** Simplify $\frac{x^2-3x+9}{x^3+27} + \frac{x-2}{x^2+4x+3} - \frac{1}{x-2}$

**Note:** Restrictions $x \neq -3; x \neq -1$ and $x \neq 2$.

**Solution:**
1. Factor the denominators:
   - $x^3+27 = (x+3)(x^2-3x+9)$
   - $x^2+4x+3 = (x+3)(x+1)$
2. Simplify the first term: $\frac{x^2-3x+9}{(x+3)(x^2-3x+9)} = \frac{1}{x+3}$
3. Combine the fractions:
$$\frac{1}{x+3} + \frac{x-2}{(x+3)(x+1)} - \frac{1}{x-2}$$
$$= \frac{(x+1)(x-2) + (x-2)^2 - (x+3)(x+1)}{(x+3)(x+1)(x-2)}$$
$$= \frac{x^2-x-2 + x^2-4x+4 - (x^2+4x+3)}{(x+3)(x+1)(x-2)}$$
$$= \frac{x^2-x-2 + x^2-4x+4 - x^2-4x-3}{(x+3)(x+1)(x-2)}$$
$$= \frac{x^2-9x-1}{(x+3)(x+1)(x-2)}$$

---

### Algebraic Fractions: Simplification and Restrictions

#### Worked Example: Simplifying Complex Expressions
**Problem:** Simplify the following expression:
$$\frac{1}{a^2 - 4ab + 4b^2} + \frac{a^2 + 2ab + b^2}{a^3 - 8b^3} - \frac{1}{a^2 - 4b^2}$$

**Solution:**
1. Factorize the denominators and numerators:
   $$\frac{1}{(a - 2b)(a - 2b)} + \frac{a^2 + 2ab + b^2}{(a - 2b)(a^2 + 2ab + 4b^2)} - \frac{1}{(a - 2b)(a + 2b)}$$

2. Find the common denominator and combine:
   $$= \frac{(a + 2b) + (a - 2b)(a + 2b) - (a - 2b)}{(a - 2b)^2(a + 2b)}$$
   *(Note: The middle term simplifies based on the common denominator structure)*

3. Expand and simplify the numerator:
   $$= \frac{a + 2b + a^2 - 4b^2 - a + 2b}{(a - 2b)^2(a + 2b)}$$
   $$= \frac{a^2 + 4b - 4b^2}{(a - 2b)^2(a + 2b)}$$

**Restriction:** $a \neq \pm 2b$

---

#### Concept: Determining Restrictions
Restrictions occur when the denominator of a fraction equals zero, as division by zero is undefined. To find restrictions, set the denominator equal to zero and solve for the variable.

**Example 4a:**
Find the restriction for: $\frac{1}{x - 2}$
*   Set denominator to zero: $x - 2 = 0$
*   Solve: $x = 2$
*   **Restriction:** $x \neq 2$

**Example 4b:**
Find the restriction for: $\frac{3x - 9}{4x + 4}$
1. Simplify the fraction:
   $$\frac{3(x - 1)}{4(x + 1)}$$
2. Set the denominator to zero:
   $$4(x + 1) = 0$$
   $$x + 1 = 0$$
   $$x = -1$$
*   **Restriction:** $x \neq -1$

**Example 4c:**
Find the restrictions for: $\frac{3}{x} - \frac{1}{x^2 - 1}$
1. Factorize the denominator:
   $$\frac{3}{x} - \frac{1}{(x - 1)(x + 1)}$$
2. Identify all values that make any denominator zero:
   *   $x = 0$
   *   $x - 1 = 0 \implies x = 1$
   *   $x + 1 = 0 \implies x = -1$
*   **Restrictions:** $x \neq 0$ and $x \neq \pm 1$

# 1.9 Chapter Summary: Number Systems

## 1. Venn Diagram of Number Sets
The following diagram illustrates the relationship between the special sets of numbers: Natural numbers ($\mathbb{N}$), Whole numbers ($\mathbb{N}_0$), and Integers ($\mathbb{Z}$).

<!-- DIAGRAM_PLACEHOLDER: diagram -->

### Worked Example: Categorizing Numbers
**Question:** Where does the number $2,13$ belong in the diagram?

**Solution:**
The number $2,13$ is in its simplest form (a decimal). It is not a member of $\mathbb{N}$, $\mathbb{N}_0$, or $\mathbb{Z}$. Therefore, it is located in the space outside the circles but inside the rectangle $X$.

---

## 2. Properties of Number Sets
**Question:** Identify the true statement among the following:
*   (i) Every natural number is an integer.
*   (ii) Every whole number is a natural number.
*   (iii) There are fractions in the integers.

**Solution:**
*   **Statement (i):** Integers consist of natural numbers and negative natural numbers. This statement is **true**.
*   **Statement (ii):** $0$ is a whole number but not a natural number. This statement is **false**.
*   **Statement (iii):** Integers do not contain fractions. This statement is **false**.

**Conclusion:** The only true statement is (i).

---

## 3. Real, Non-real, and Undefined Numbers
Numbers can be classified based on their properties under square roots and division.

### Worked Examples
**a) Determine the nature of $-\sqrt{-5}$**
*   **Solution:** This is the square root of a negative number. Therefore, it is **non-real**.

**b) Determine the nature of $\frac{\sqrt{8}}{0}$**
*   **Solution:** Division by zero is mathematically impossible. Therefore, this expression is **undefined**.

**c) Determine the nature of $-\sqrt{15}$**
*   **Solution:** This is the square root of a positive number. Therefore, it is **real**.

---

### Real and Non-Real Numbers

#### Identifying Real vs. Non-Real Numbers
A square root of a negative number is non-real. A square root of a positive number is real.

*   **Example d:** $-\sqrt{7}$
    *   **Solution:** This is the square root of a positive number and is **real**.
*   **Example e:** $\sqrt{-1}$
    *   **Solution:** This is the square root of a negative number and is **non-real**.
*   **Example f:** $\sqrt{2}$
    *   **Solution:** This is the square root of a positive number and is **real**.

---

### Rational vs. Irrational Numbers
A number is **rational** if it can be expressed as a fraction of two integers. Otherwise, it is **irrational**.

**Exercises:**
1.  **$\sqrt[3]{4}$**: Irrational (cannot be simplified to a fraction of integers).
2.  **$45\pi$**: Irrational (cannot be simplified to a fraction of integers).
3.  **$\sqrt{9}$**: $\sqrt{9} = 3$. Rational (can be simplified to an integer).
4.  **$\sqrt[3]{8}$**: $\sqrt[3]{8} = 2$. Rational (can be simplified to an integer).

---

### Properties of Rational and Irrational Numbers
Given: $a$ is an integer, $b$ is an integer, and $c$ is irrational.

| Expression | Classification | Reasoning |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| $\frac{-b}{a}$ | Rational | A fraction of integers is rational. |
| $c \div c$ | Rational | Dividing a number by itself equals $1$. |
| $\frac{a}{c}$ | Irrational | Dividing an integer by an irrational number is irrational (unless $a=0$, then it is $0$, which is rational). |
| $\frac{1}{c}$ | Irrational | Dividing an integer by an irrational number is irrational. |

### Number Systems and Conversions

#### Analysis of Number Types
Based on the provided values, we categorize numbers into specific sets:

*   **Irrational Numbers:** Any number that cannot be written as a fraction of two integers.
    *   Examples: $-\sqrt{24}$, $\frac{\pi}{2}$, $\sqrt[3]{26}$, $\pi$, $\sqrt{39}$, $\sqrt{78}$, $3\pi$, $\pi^2$.
*   **Rational Numbers:** Any number that can be written as a fraction of two integers.
    *   Examples: $\frac{3}{2}$, $7,11$, $7,12$, $9$.
*   **Integers:** Whole numbers (positive, negative, or zero).
    *   Example: $9$.
*   **Undefined Expressions:** Any fraction with a denominator of $0$.
    *   Example: $\frac{\sqrt{2}}{0}$.

#### 6. Converting Decimals to Simple Fractions

To convert decimals to fractions, follow these worked examples:

**a) Convert $0,12$ to a fraction**
$$0,12 = \frac{1}{10} + \frac{2}{100}$$
$$= \frac{12}{100}$$
$$= \frac{3}{25}$$

**b) Convert $0,006$ to a fraction**
$$0,006 = \frac{6}{1000}$$
$$= \frac{3}{500}$$

**c) Convert $4,\overline{14}$ to a fraction**
Let $x = 4,141414...$
Multiply by $100$ to shift the repeating decimal:
$$100x = 414,141414...$$
Subtract the original $x$:
$$100x - x = (414,141414...) - (4,141414...)$$
$$99x = 410$$
$$\therefore x = \frac{410}{99}$$

**d) Convert $1,59$ to a fraction**
$$1,59 = 1 + \frac{5}{10} + \frac{9}{100}$$
$$= 1 \frac{59}{100}$$
$$= \frac{159}{100}$$

<!-- DIAGRAM_PLACEHOLDER: diagram -->

### Converting Recurring Decimals to Fractions

To convert a recurring decimal into a fraction, we use algebraic manipulation to eliminate the repeating part.

#### Worked Example: e) $12,27\dot{7}$
Let $x = 12,27\dot{7}$
Multiply by $10$ to shift the decimal: $10x = 122,7\dot{7}$
Multiply by $100$ to shift the decimal: $100x = 1227,7\dot{7}$
Subtract the equations:
$$100x - 10x = 1227,7\dot{7} - 122,7\dot{7}$$
$$90x = 1105$$
$$x = \frac{1105}{90}$$
Simplify the fraction:
$$x = \frac{221}{18}$$

#### Worked Example: f) $0,8\dot{2}$
Let $x = 0,8222...$
Multiply by $10$: $10x = 8,222...$
Multiply by $100$: $100x = 82,222...$
Subtract the equations:
$$100x - 10x = 82,222... - 8,222...$$
$$90x = 74$$
$$x = \frac{74}{90}$$
Simplify the fraction:
$$x = \frac{37}{45}$$

#### Worked Example: g) $7,\overline{36}$
Let $x = 7,363636...$
Multiply by $100$: $100x = 736,363636...$
Subtract $x$ from $100x$:
$$100x - x = 736,363636... - 7,363636...$$
$$99x = 729$$
$$x = \frac{729}{99}$$
Simplify the fraction:
$$x = \frac{81}{11}$$

### Proving Rational Numbers

A number is **rational** if it can be expressed in the form $\frac{a}{b}$ where $a$ and $b$ are integers and $b \neq 0$.

#### Worked Example: Show that $3,21\dot{1}\dot{8}$ is a rational number.
Let $x = 3,211818...$
Multiply by $1000$: $1000x = 3211,818181...$
Multiply by $10000$: $10000x = 32118,181818...$
Subtract the equations:
$$10000x - 1000x = 32118,181818... - 3211,818181...$$
$$9000x = 28906,36...$$
*(Note: Based on the provided text logic)*:
$$10000x - x = 32115$$
$$9999x = 32115$$
$$x = \frac{32115}{9999}$$

**Conclusion:** This is a rational number because both the numerator and denominator are integers.

### Converting Fractions to Decimals

To convert a fraction to a decimal, perform long division of the numerator by the denominator.

**Example: Convert $1\frac{1}{2}$ to a decimal**

1. Convert the mixed number to an improper fraction:
   $$1\frac{1}{2} = \frac{3}{2}$$
2. Perform long division:
   $$2 \mid 3,0000 = 1 \text{ remainder } 1$$
   $$2 \mid 3,^10000 = 5 \text{ remainder } 0$$
   Result: $1,5$

---

### Converting Recurring Decimals to Fractions

To express a recurring decimal as a fraction $\frac{a}{b}$ (where $a, b \in \mathbb{Z}$), use algebraic manipulation.

**Example: Express $0,\overline{78}$ as a fraction**

1. Let $x = 0,\overline{78}$
2. Multiply by $100$ (since two digits repeat):
   $$100x = 78,\overline{78}$$
3. Subtract the original equation:
   $$100x - x = 78$$
   $$99x = 78$$
4. Solve for $x$:
   $$x = \frac{78}{99}$$

---

### Identifying Rational and Irrational Numbers

*   **Rational Numbers:** Can be expressed as a fraction. They either terminate or have a repeating pattern of digits.
*   **Irrational Numbers:** Cannot be expressed as a fraction. They do not terminate and have no repeating pattern.

**Exercises:**

| Number | Next three digits | Classification | Reason |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| $1,11235...$ | Indeterminate | Irrational | No repeating pattern and does not terminate. |
| $1,\dot{1}$ | $111$ | Rational | There is a repeating pattern of digits. |

---

### Rounding Rational Numbers

To round a number to a specific number of decimal places, identify the cut-off point and determine if the following digit requires rounding up.

**Examples:**

*   **a) $\frac{1}{2}$ to 2 decimal places:**
    Convert to decimal: $\frac{1}{2} = 0,50$
*   **b) $1$ to 2 decimal places:**
    Add a comma and two zeros: $1,00$
*   **c) $0,11111\dot{1}$ to 2 decimal places:**
    The digit after the cut-off is $1$, so do not round up.
    Result: $0,11111\dot{1} \approx 0,11$
*   **d) $0,99999\dot{1}$ to 2 decimal places:**
    The digit after the cut-off is $9$, so round up.
    Result: $0,99999\dot{1} \approx 1,00$

<!-- DIAGRAM_PLACEHOLDER: diagram -->

---

### Rounding Irrational Numbers

Rounding is the process of approximating a number to a specific degree of accuracy (decimal places). When rounding, look at the digit immediately to the right of the required decimal place:
*   If the digit is $5$ or greater, round up.
*   If the digit is less than $5$, keep the digit as is.

#### Worked Examples: Rounding to 3 Decimal Places
12. Round the following irrational numbers to 3 decimal places:

| Number | Calculation/Logic | Result |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| a) $3,141592654...$ | Digit after 3rd decimal is 5, round up. | $3,142$ |
| b) $1,618033989...$ | Digit after 3rd decimal is 0, no change. | $1,618$ |
| c) $1,41421356...$ | Digit after 3rd decimal is 2, no change. | $1,414$ |
| d) $2,718281828...$ | Digit after 3rd decimal is 2, no change. | $2,718$ |

### 21. Estimating Surds
To estimate the value of a surd to the nearest decimal place without a calculator, identify the two perfect squares between which the radicand lies.

**a) $\sqrt{14}$**
*   **Solution:** $\sqrt{14}$ lies between $\sqrt{9}$ ($3^2$) and $\sqrt{16}$ ($4^2$). Since $14$ is closer to $16$ than to $9$, the estimate is $3,7$ or $3,8$.

**b) $\sqrt{110}$**
*   **Solution:** $\sqrt{110}$ lies between $\sqrt{100}$ ($10^2$) and $\sqrt{121}$ ($11^2$). Since $110$ is almost exactly between $100$ and $121$, the estimate is $10,5$.

**c) $\sqrt{48}$**
*   **Solution:** $\sqrt{48}$ lies between $\sqrt{36}$ ($6^2$) and $\sqrt{49}$ ($7^2$). Since $48$ is much closer to $49$, the estimate is $6,9$.

**d) $\sqrt{57}$**
*   **Solution:** $\sqrt{57}$ lies between $\sqrt{49}$ ($7^2$) and $\sqrt{64}$ ($8^2$). Since $57$ is almost exactly between $49$ and $64$, the estimate is $4,5$ or $4,6$.

### 22. Expanding Algebraic Products
Use the distributive property (FOIL method) to expand the following expressions.

**a) $(a + 5)^2$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$(a + 5)(a + 5) = a^2 + 5a + 5a + 25 = a^2 + 10a + 25$$

**b) $(n + 12)^2$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$(n + 12)(n + 12) = n^2 + 12n + 12n + 144 = n^2 + 24n + 144$$

**c) $(d - 4)^2$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$(d - 4)(d - 4) = d^2 - 4d - 4d + 16 = d^2 - 8d + 16$$

**d) $(7w + 2)(7w - 2)$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$(7w + 2)(7w - 2) = 49w^2 - 14w + 14w - 4 = 49w^2 - 4$$

**e) $(12q + 1)(12q - 1)$**
*   **Solution:**
    $$(12q + 1)(12q - 1) = 144q^2 - 12q + 12q - 1 = 144q^2 - 1$$

---

### Algebraic Expansion: Worked Examples

This section provides step-by-step solutions for the expansion of binomial products.

---

#### f) Expand: $-( -x - 2)(x + 2)$
**Solution:**
$$-( -x - 2)(x + 2) = (x + 2)(x + 2)$$
$$= x^2 + 2x + 2x + 4$$
$$= x^2 + 4x + 4$$

#### g) Expand: $(5k - 4)(5k + 4)$
**Solution:**
$$(5k - 4)(5k + 4) = 25k^2 + 20k - 20k - 16$$
$$= 25k^2 - 16$$

#### h) Expand: $(5f + 4)(2f + 2)$
**Solution:**
$$(5f + 4)(2f + 2) = 10f^2 + 10f + 8f + 8$$
$$= 10f^2 + 18f + 8$$

#### i) Expand: $(3n + 6)(6n + 5)$
**Solution:**
$$(3n + 6)(6n + 5) = 18n^2 + 15n + 36n + 30$$
$$= 18n^2 + 51n + 30$$

#### j) Expand: $(2g + 6)(g + 6)$
**Solution:**
$$(2g + 6)(g + 6) = 2g^2 + 12g + 6g + 36$$
$$= 2g^2 + 18g + 36$$

#### k) Expand: $(4y + 1)(4y + 8)$
**Solution:**
$$(4y + 1)(4y + 8) = 16y^2 + 32y + 4y + 8$$
$$= 16y^2 + 36y + 8$$

#### l) Expand: $(d - 3)(7d + 2)$
**Solution:**
$$(d - 3)(7d + 2) = 7d^2 + 2d - 21d - 6$$
$$= 7d^2 - 19d - 6$$

#### m) Expand: $(6z - 4)(z - 2)$
**Solution:**
$$(6z - 4)(z - 2) = 6z^2 - 12z - 4z + 8$$
$$= 6z^2 - 16z + 8$$

#### n) Expand: $(5w - 11)^2$
**Solution:**
$$(5w - 11)^2 = (5w - 11)(5w - 11)$$
$$= 25w^2 - 55w - 55w + 121$$
$$= 25w^2 - 110w + 121$$

---

### Algebraic Expressions: Expansion and Simplification

This section focuses on the expansion of algebraic expressions, including squaring binomials, distributing terms, and multiplying polynomials.

---

#### 1. Squaring Binomials
To square a binomial, multiply the expression by itself and combine like terms.

**o) Expand $(5s - 1)^2$**
$$(5s - 1)^2 = (5s - 1)(5s - 1)$$
$$= 25s^2 - 5s - 5s + 1$$
$$= 25s^2 - 10s + 1$$

**p) Expand $(3d - 8)^2$**
$$(3d - 8)^2 = (3d - 8)(3d - 8)$$
$$= 9d^2 - 24d - 24d + 64$$
$$= 9d^2 - 48d + 64$$

---

#### 2. Distributive Property and Combining Like Terms
Expand the terms inside the brackets and then group terms with the same variables and exponents.

**q) Expand $5f^2(3f + 5) + 7f(3f^2 + 7)$**
$$5f^2(3f + 5) + 7f(3f^2 + 7) = 15f^3 + 25f^2 + 21f^3 + 49f$$
$$= 36f^3 + 25f^2 + 49f$$

**r) Expand $8d(4d^3 + 2) + 6d^2(7d^2 + 4)$**
$$8d(4d^3 + 2) + 6d^2(7d^2 + 4) = 32d^4 + 16d + 42d^4 + 24d^2$$
$$= 74d^4 + 24d^2 + 16d$$

**s) Expand $5x^2(2x + 2) + 7x(7x^2 + 7)$**
$$5x^2(2x + 2) + 7x(7x^2 + 7) = 10x^3 + 10x^2 + 49x^3 + 49x$$
$$= 59x^3 + 10x^2 + 49x$$

---

#### 3. Advanced Polynomial Expansion
These exercises involve multiplying multiple brackets and simplifying complex expressions.

**23. a) Expand $(y^4 + 3y^2 + y)(y + 1)(y - 2)$**
$$(y^4 + 3y^2 + y)(y + 1)(y - 2) = (y^4 + 3y^2 + y)(y^2 - y - 2)$$
$$= y^6 - y^5 - 2y^4 + 3y^4 - 3y^3 - 6y^2 + y^3 - y^2 - 2y$$
$$= y^6 - y^5 + y^4 - 2y^3 - 7y^2 - 2y$$

**b) Expand $(x + 1)^2 - (x - 1)^2$**
$$(x + 1)^2 - (x - 1)^2 = (x^2 + 2x + 1) - (x^2 - 2x + 1)$$
$$= x^2 + 2x + 1 - x^2 + 2x - 1$$
$$= 4x$$

**c) Expand $(x^2 + 2x + 1)(x^2 - 2x + 1)$**
$$(x^2 + 2x + 1)(x^2 - 2x + 1) = x^4 - 2x^3 + x^2 + 2x^3 - 4x^2 + 2x + x^2 - 2x + 1$$
$$= x^4 - 2x^2 + 1$$

---

### Algebraic Expansion and Simplification

This section covers the expansion of algebraic expressions, including binomials, trinomials, and the application of difference of squares and distributive laws.

---

#### Worked Examples

**d) Expand $(4a - 3b)(16a^2 + 12ab + 9b^2)$**
$$(4a - 3b)(16a^2 + 12ab + 9b^2) = 64a^3 + 48a^2b + 36ab^2 - 48a^2b - 36ab^2 - 27b^3$$
$$= 64a^3 - 27b^3$$

**e) Expand $2(x + 3y)(x^2 - xy - y^2)$**
$$2(x + 3y)(x^2 - xy - y^2) = 2(x^3 - x^2y - xy^2 + 3x^2y - 3xy^2 - 3y^3)$$
$$= 2(x^3 + 2x^2y - 4xy^2 - 3y^3)$$
$$= 2x^3 + 4x^2y - 8xy^2 - 6y^3$$

**f) Expand $(3a - 5b)(3a + 5b)(a^2 + ab - b^2)$**
$$(3a - 5b)(3a + 5b)(a^2 + ab - b^2) = (9a^2 - 25b^2)(a^2 + ab - b^2)$$
$$= 9a^4 + 9a^3b - 9a^2b^2 - 25a^2b^2 - 25ab^3 + 25b^4$$
$$= 9a^4 + 9a^3b - 34a^2b^2 - 25ab^3 + 25b^4$$

**g) Expand $(y - \frac{1}{y})(y + \frac{1}{y})$**
$$(y - \frac{1}{y})(y + \frac{1}{y}) = y^2 + 1 - 1 - \frac{1}{y^2}$$
$$= y^2 - \frac{1}{y^2}$$

**h) Expand $(\frac{a}{3} - \frac{3}{a})(\frac{a}{3} + \frac{3}{a})$**
$$(\frac{a}{3} - \frac{3}{a})(\frac{a}{3} + \frac{3}{a}) = \frac{a^2}{9} + 1 - 1 - \frac{9}{a^2}$$
$$= \frac{a^2}{9} - \frac{9}{a^2}$$

**i) Simplify $\frac{1}{3}(12x - 9y) + \frac{1}{6}(12x + 18y)$**
$$\frac{1}{3}(12x - 9y) + \frac{1}{6}(12x + 18y) = 4x - 3y + 2x + 3y$$
$$= 6x$$

**j) Simplify $(x + 2)(x - 2) - (x + 2)^2$**
$$(x + 2)(x - 2) - (x + 2)^2 = (x^2 - 4) - (x^2 + 4x + 4)$$
$$= x^2 - 4 - x^2 - 4x - 4$$
$$= -4x - 8$$

---

#### Solving for Unknowns

**24. Find the value of $e$ in $(x - 4)(x + e) = x^2 - 16$**

**Solution:**
Expand the left-hand side:
$$(x - 4)(x + e) = x^2 + ex - 4x - 4e$$
$$= x^2 + (e - 4)x - 4e$$

Compare this to the given expression $x^2 - 16$:
1. The coefficient of $x$ is $0$, so $e - 4 = 0 \implies e = 4$.
2. The constant term is $-4e = -16 \implies e = 4$.

Therefore, **$e = 4$**.

### 25. Algebraic Expressions and Parameters

Given the expression: $(x + 2)(x + k) = x^2 + bx + c$

First, expand the expression:
$$(x + 2)(x + k) = x^2 + kx + 2x + 2k$$
$$= x^2 + (k + 2)x + 2k$$

From this, we identify:
*   The coefficient of $x$ (the $b$ term) is $k + 2$.
*   The constant term (the $c$ term) is $2k$.

#### Exercises
**a) For which of these values of $k$ will $b$ be positive?**
Values: $-6; -1; 0; 1; 6$
*   **Solution:** Since $b = k + 2$, we need $k + 2 > 0$, which means $k > -2$.
*   From the given list, the values are: $-1; 0; 1; 6$.

**b) For which of these values of $k$ will $c$ be positive?**
Values: $-6; -1; 0; 1; 6$
*   **Solution:** Since $c = 2k$, we need $2k > 0$, which means $k > 0$.
*   From the given list, the values are: $1; 6$.

**c) For what values of $k$ will $c$ be positive?**
*   **Solution:** $2k > 0 \implies k > 0$.

**d) For what values of $k$ will $b$ be positive?**
*   **Solution:** $k + 2 > 0 \implies k > -2$.

---

### 26. Expansion and Factorization of Cubes

#### a) Expand: $\left(3a - \frac{1}{2a}\right)^2$
*   **Solution:**
$$\left(3a - \frac{1}{2a}\right)^2 = (3a)^2 - 2(3a)\left(\frac{1}{2a}\right) + \left(\frac{1}{2a}\right)^2$$
$$= 9a^2 - 3 + \frac{1}{4a^2}$$

#### b) Expand: $\left(3a - \frac{1}{2a}\right)\left(9a^2 + \frac{3}{2} + \frac{1}{4a^2}\right)$
*   **Solution:**
$$\left(3a - \frac{1}{2a}\right)\left(9a^2 + \frac{3}{2} + \frac{1}{4a^2}\right) = 27a^3 + \frac{9}{2}a + \frac{3}{4a} - \frac{9}{2}a - \frac{3}{4a} - \frac{1}{8a^3}$$
$$= 27a^3 - \frac{1}{8a^3}$$

#### c) Given that $3a - \frac{1}{2a} = 7$, determine the value of $27a^3 - \frac{1}{8a^3}$ without solving for $a$.
*   **Solution:**
Using the result from (b):
$$27a^3 - \frac{1}{8a^3} = \left(3a - \frac{1}{2a}\right)\left(9a^2 + \frac{3}{2} + \frac{1}{4a^2}\right)$$
Substitute $3a - \frac{1}{2a} = 7$:
$$= 7\left(9a^2 + \frac{3}{2} + \frac{1}{4a^2}\right)$$
To find $9a^2 + \frac{1}{4a^2}$, square the given expression:
$$\left(3a - \frac{1}{2a}\right)^2 = 7^2$$
$$9a^2 - 3 + \frac{1}{4a^2} = 49$$
$$9a^2 + \frac{1}{4a^2} = 52$$
Now substitute back into the expansion:
$$27a^3 - \frac{1}{8a^3} = 7\left(52 + \frac{3}{2}\right)$$
$$= 7\left(\frac{104}{2} + \frac{3}{2}\right) = 7\left(\frac{107}{2}\right)$$
$$= \frac{749}{2} = 374\frac{1}{2}$$

---

### 27. Solve by factorising
This section uses the difference of two squares identity: $a^2 - b^2 = (a - b)(a + b)$.

**a) $17^2 - 15^2$**
$$17^2 - 15^2 = (17 - 15)(17 + 15)$$
$$= 2(32)$$
$$= 64$$

**b) $13^2 - 12^2$**
$$13^2 - 12^2 = (13 - 12)(13 + 12)$$
$$= 1(25)$$
$$= 25$$

**c) $120045^2 - 120035^2$**
$$120045^2 - 120035^2 = (120045 - 120035)(120045 + 120035)$$
$$= 10(240080)$$
$$= 2400800$$

**d) $26^2 - 24^2$**
$$26^2 - 24^2 = (26 - 24)(26 + 24)$$
$$= 2(50)$$
$$= 100$$

### 28. Represent as a product of prime factors
This section uses the difference of squares method to factorise numbers into their prime components.

**a) $143$**
$$143 = 144 - 1$$
$$= (12 - 1)(12 + 1)$$
$$= 11 \times 13$$

**b) $168$**
$$168 = 169 - 1$$
$$= (13 - 1)(13 + 1)$$
$$= 12 \times 14$$
$$= (3 \times 2^2) \times (2 \times 7)$$
$$= 2^3 \times 3 \times 7$$

**c) $899$**
$$899 = 900 - 1$$
$$= (30 - 1)(30 + 1)$$
$$= 29 \times 31$$
